The 1.05× timing is the strongest completed maximum-fanin schematic result so far, but it does not qualify the synapse: the event lookup is still calibrated at40.253ns, the R-2R ladder is represented by an ideal effective gate correction, and the result covers one SS/0°C site with no mismatch, noise, threshold/reset feedback, service timing or extracted layout. Next measure the single-event geometry/bias and DAC transfer at42.266ns, rebuild the finite float mapping, then re-run signed fan-in and cancellation/order checks before considering another state architecture.

### Matched bank-loaded continuous map and duration-floor probe — 2026-09-30

The finite geometry table was recalibrated with the full shared 13-geometry bank connected, inactive branches held off, and the deployed 42.2657ns event pulse. This removes a load mismatch between isolated calibration and the actual shared-bank fan-in. A continuous analog gate-bias inversion of the measured bank-loaded curves gives a 370-event float lookup sum of−52.45648mV versus−52.45418mV target (−0.0044%). Separate physical PDK MOS/TG/MIM shared-bank sequences at SS/0°C deliver−51.58811/−52.09683/−51.82068mV in forward/reverse/shuffled order: errors−1.651/−0.681/−1.208%, with0.509mV maximum order spread. This is the best completed maximum-fanin result in this branch. The event deck applies a measured TT R-2R correction as ideal PWL gate commands; it does not instantiate the ladder.

A continuous-bias single-event map predicts−0.928mV versus−0.924mV physical for one event, but a 50-event signed prefix remains +7.8% high. Thus single-event and total-sum agreement do not bound state/order-dependent accumulation. At the near-threshold final-fusion site (329 events, +32.623uV float sum), adding a duration-coded floor to the smallest measured geometry reduces the mapped sum error to+0.083%; physical forward/reverse/shuffled runs are+33.047/+32.951/+33.006uV, errors+1.30/+1.00/+1.18%. The floor uses 2.5ns minimum pulses and 0.5ns edges; 13 events remain below the measured floor. At the high-cancellation site (322 events, only+7.491nV ideal), the mapped sum is+121.484nV and physical order results are+0.232/+0.142/+0.192uV. Relative error is not meaningful at this near-zero target; report absolute residual and 89.5nV order span. These residuals are below the previously estimated ~200uV thermal-noise proxy, so this circuit cannot claim robust near-zero decisions.

This is schematic device evidence only: no physical resistor ladder in full fan-in, no transistor-level presynaptic event capture, comparator/threshold/reset/beta feedback, noise/mismatch/PVT sweep on the complete path, extraction/layout or full-network spike/task equivalence. Full float32 weights are used without QAT. Keep the shared-bank continuous map provisional. Next qualify representative sites with physical threshold/readout/reset and state feedback, including near-zero noise margin; then broaden output/site coverage before layout.

### Same-site threshold-margin sensitivity — 2026-09-30

Added `scripts/analyze_sky130_ms_fanin_threshold_margins.py` to compare the saved full-float decisions against the three measured physical fan-in orders at the exact trace indices used for the max-fanin, near-threshold and high-cancellation screens. It adds the physical event-sum error to the same-site float candidate in model units and compares against the saved actor threshold. All three sampled sites remain non-spiking for every measured event order; none of these residuals alone changes the saved decision. At the near-threshold and high-cancellation sites, however, the float margins map to only−4.09uV and−5.77uV respectively, while the prior thermal-noise proxy is about200uV. Therefore the deterministic, no-noise decision match does not establish reliable threshold operation. The max-fanin case is far from threshold and is a weak sensitivity test.

This is a pointwise perturbation analysis: beta/prior state, threshold, upstream events and neuron state remain from the float trace. There is no physical threshold comparator, feedback, noise, mismatch or task-level replay. The result shows that the injection fit is not the remaining evidence gate; physical state/readout noise and closed-loop PLIF behavior are. Continue with analog correlated/auto-zero readout or another low-disturbance threshold method, quantify physical decision margin over time, and replay a held-out trace before layout.

### Continuous OTA charge bucket with switched neutral reference — 2026-09-30

The older `sky130_sc_integrator_many_events` no-event subtraction proved sensitive to the composition of independent circuits in one ngspice deck. Re-ran 100 fixed ±0.3V packet events over a5ms TT/27°C window, using a physical1µm MIM packet,100µm MIM feedback, continuously biased single-ended SKY130 OTA, and an additional physically switched zero-charge branch. Separate three-branch decks yield final signal-minus-neutral −3.6614mV for100 positive events and+3.7434mV for100 negative events. Fifty positive/fifty negative events yield+9.1µV in p-first order and+6.8µV in n-first order. These are nominal control-subtracted transfer measurements, not hardware threshold decisions. The balanced residuals already exceed the4.09–5.77µV mapped margins of two sampled final-fusion sites.

The separate neutral OTA is sensitive to its bias: changing only its n-tail ideal bias by+0.1mV moves the balanced signal-minus-neutral output from+9.1µV to−168.3µV; +1mV gives+405.9µV. These deterministic stresses are not statistical mismatch/yield, but the topology does not demonstrate robust offset cancellation. The no-event reference also chooses different nominal operating points in otherwise independent decks: in the four-branch run it differs from event branches by~0.216mV before the first event and by~2mV after5ms. A100µs physical VCM clamp alone sends output toward0.49V rather than maintaining the intended0.9V state. Adding switched1MΩ startup feedback until450µs makes one isolated mixed/neutral deck settle near0.898V before events and gives−1.3µV final residual, but the same mixed branch in the complete five-branch deck starts28.1µV away from neutral and ends+1.983mV away. With this startup path, +0.1mV neutral-bias stress gives+297.8µV. The cause of the deck-composition dependence has not been isolated to a device or solver mechanism; it invalidates using the isolated nominal cancellation as a circuit pass.

Reject this **single-ended continuous OTA plus separate neutral-reference branch as presently drawn** for a few-microvolt PLIF decision. The next active-bucket exploration needs a common differential front end with explicit common-mode control and correlated offset handling, plus an independent dense analog state store. That is a design direction, not a passed schematic. No actual float weights, state transfer/reconnect, thermal noise, mismatch distribution, threshold/reset/beta, PVT, extraction or 30,976,000-state service is qualified. Machine-readable evidence is `continuous_ota_charge_bucket_neutral_reference_screen` in `sky130_readout_architecture_boundary.json` and the `_neutral*` result reports.

### Actual float-weighted fan-in connected to a clocked MIM latch — 2026-09-30

Connected the existing 329-event shuffled final-fusion MOS/TG/10µm-MIM sequence to the transistor clocked latch, at SS/0°C. The original epoch58 float32 site is `[0,1,7,42,1]`. Its learned threshold is imposed as an *ideal initial differential voltage* of −36.710µV before events; that is a boundary condition, not a physical threshold DAC or PLIF feedback path. With the model threshold, the float margin is −4.087µV, the measured pre-evaluation physical state is −3.767µV, and the latch correctly gives no spike. The read TG opens 2ns after latch evaluation; the measured state shift relative to the no-event branch is +0.337µV across the latch window, including any ordinary hold drift.

Two bounded diagnostic threshold shifts test both polarities and the boundary. Lowering the threshold by 10µV yields a +5.913µV float margin, +5.987µV physical pre-evaluation state, and a spike, with −0.536µV apparent state change across the latch window. Lowering it by 4µV yields a −0.087µV float margin but +0.134µV physical pre-evaluation state and a spike. The latter is a deterministic float decision mismatch caused by only +0.222µV combined event/state error at the boundary; the latch follows the physical input in all three cases. These shifted thresholds are sensitivity probes, **not** the trained model.

The prior measured 10µm MIM capacitance estimate, 203.8fF per side, implies about 192µV RMS for two independent reset samples at 0°C by `sqrt(2kT/C)`. This is an analytic proxy, not simulated latch or reset noise, but it is roughly 47 times the model site's 4.087µV margin. The nominal correct decision therefore does not qualify reliable neuron operation. The full fan-in still uses ideal effective gate PWL commands based on measured R-2R correction, with no ladder in the deck. There is no physical threshold source, beta/prior state, subtractive reset, adaptation, switched-noise characterization, mismatch, full 5ms evolution, extracted layout or full-network replay. Evidence: `scripts/test_sky130_ms_weighted_threshold_latch.py`, the three `sky130_ms_weighted_threshold_latch_nearthr_shift{0,m4,m10}uv.json` reports, and `weighted_fanin_clocked_latch_screen` in the architecture boundary report. Continue the shared differential/correlated-offset and dense analog-state research gate; do not promote this direct MIM latch to a PLIF macro.

### Full-float state range versus 10µm-pair noise — 2026-09-30

Screened all25 PLIF actors on the frozen original-float64×64 trace, scaling each actor's maximum absolute candidate to an ideal ±0.9V range. The final-fusion candidate reaches13,800.997 model units, so this range gives65.21µV per model unit. Its absolute threshold-distance percentiles are0.537µV at0.1%,5.281µV at1%,54.49µV at5%;7.143% of its sampled candidate margins are below the independent10µm-pair192.38µV RMS `kT/C` proxy at0°C. This is a one-sigma comparison, not a computed spike-error probability. Increasing linear gain by10× would put18.06% of its observed candidate values outside the same voltage range; at32× the fraction is56.81%. Across all25 actors weighted by sampled states,2.332% of margins are below the proxy and16.52% of candidate values exceed range at10× gain. These are frozen trace/range counts, not physical voltage waveforms.

Replayed ideal symmetric state saturation through the full original float32 SNN, with QAT disabled. For each selected full validation frame, each actor was given its own *per-record* maximum as the ideal ±0.9V baseline; candidate and post-subtractive-reset membrane were clipped when gain increased. A unity-gain zero-noise replay exactly matched float. With all25 actors and no noise, gain10× changes about0.22–0.26% of final-fusion spikes and1.7–1.9% of pixel classes on records0/1; gain32× changes1.343% of spikes and8.5/8.2% of pixel classes; gain64× changes2.342% and13.15/12.64%; gain100× collapses to13.79/14.27% spike mismatch and only46.46/44.63% pixel agreement. The corresponding baseline present-class mean IoUs are0.20573/0.19892. Gain32× and64× can show similar or slightly higher mean IoU on these two records despite substantial prediction disagreement, so the IoU change is not evidence of equivalence or generalization.

Added an independent Gaussian 10µm-pair reset-noise *software proxy* at initialization and after spikes, preserving subtractive reset, over three seeds per frame. At unity gain, mean final-fusion spike mismatch is15.56/15.31% and mean pixel agreement45.50/47.03% on records0/1. At gain10× it is11.96/11.87% and61.99/62.00%; at32× it is4.60/4.93% and79.70/77.91%; at64× it is4.65/4.88% and79.00/77.52%. Gain100× again collapses to13.88/14.34% spike mismatch and46.29/44.58% pixel agreement. The32/64× mean IoUs can remain near or slightly above the two frame baselines while roughly one in five predicted pixels changes. This rejects **simple fixed linear gain plus hard saturation as a demonstrated faithful analog state solution**. It does not reject a nonlinear or split-range state representation, because neither was simulated or built.

This replay is deliberately optimistic: gains are separately normalized to each validation record's observed maximum, and ideal clipping has no hardware cost. The independent Gaussian reset sample is not measured transistor switched noise; the actual source, threshold, beta, adaptive thresholds, readout and overflow detection are not instantiated. Only two complete frames and three noise seeds were screened. A credible next analog state/readout candidate must preserve a wide accumulated-state range while giving much finer differential resolution near threshold, with physical common-mode/offset control, and must then be replayed with measured circuit errors and a fixed calibration across held-out records. Reports: `sky130_float_dynamic_range_noise_boundary.json`, `sky130_float_state_saturation_validation_{0,1}_all_neurons_full_*`, and `float_state_gain_noise_tradeoff` in `sky130_readout_architecture_boundary.json`.

### Ideal nonlinear state encoding bound — 2026-09-30

Tested a mathematical analog companding bound on the same two full float32 validation frames: `V=0.9·asinh(m/(M/R))/asinh(R)`, where `M` is each actor's *per-record* maximum absolute candidate and `R` controls how much voltage resolution moves toward zero. An exact inverse is assumed for state updates. All25 actors pass zero-noise controls exactly for `R=100,500,1000,5000,10000`, so this map itself need not quantize or clip the observed full-float state in software. The near-zero voltage gains relative to direct linear mapping are18.87×,72.38×,131.56×,542.87× and1009.75×, respectively.

Injecting the same independent10µm-pair192.38µV RMS Gaussian voltage reset-noise proxy at initialization and after subtractive spike reset, then decoding exactly, gives these three-seed means on records0/1: `R=1000` final-fusion spike mismatch3.57/3.88%, pixel agreement82.84/80.89%; `R=5000` mismatch2.67/2.82%, agreement85.96/84.99%; `R=10000` mismatch2.31/2.41%, agreement87.71/86.71%. Present-class mean IoU at `R=10000` is0.2056/0.2003 versus float0.2057/0.1989, but12–13% of pixel classes still differ, so the similar two-frame IoU is not equivalence. This ideal nonlinear bound improves on the simple clipped-gain proxy but does **not** qualify a circuit.

The missing implementation is major: a physical state-dependent signed event update would have to produce `f(m+Δm)−f(m)` for every actual float-weight event, followed by beta/leak, dynamic threshold and subtractive reset in the same encoding. At `R=10000` using record0's final-fusion maximum15,036.96 model units, an *illustrative* +1 model-unit input would require+56.69mV encoded step from zero state but only+90.83µV from state1000, a624× state-dependent ratio. The measured shared-bank direct-MOS event ceiling is16.62mV in its current42.266ns calibration, so even the +1-at-zero illustrative companded step exceeds that particular source range; these are not actual per-weight event steps. The current MOS/MIM injector produces approximately additive voltage charge and does not implement the nonlinear update. Exact analog encode/decode, per-record maximum calibration, rail limiting, comparator offset, switch noise, local mismatch, multi-million-state storage and5ms service are all idealized or absent. Continue with a physically specific split-range or charge-domain candidate only if it can retain the full float semantics without assuming this ideal transform. Evidence: `scripts/analyze_sky130_float_state_saturation_replay.py`, `sky130_float_state_saturation_validation_{0,1}_all_neurons_full_*_asinh_ideal.json`, and `ideal_asinh_state_encoding_bound` in the architecture boundary report.

An ideal **eight-range piecewise linear** version of the same `R=10000` curve also passes exact zero-noise controls on both complete frames. Under the same three-seed10µm reset-noise proxy, mean final-fusion spike mismatch is2.462/2.555% and pixel agreement87.03/86.14% on records0/1. That is close to the smooth ideal bound while reducing the mathematical encoding to eight constant slopes per polarity. Its first final-fusion record0 segment spans0–2.375 model units at47.37mV per unit, and the last spans4,360–15,037 units at10.54µV per unit: a~4,496× slope change. The first segment's illustrative +1-unit step still exceeds the measured16.62mV direct-MOS event ceiling. **No range switch or state-dependent charge transfer has been built.** This is a concrete requirement for the next bounded physical bench: show a signed event crossing one gain boundary and a full5ms hold without losing or inventing charge, then add physical comparator/reset and noise. The eight-range replay does not select a physical macro. Evidence: `sky130_float_state_saturation_validation_{0,1}_all_neurons_full_*_piecewise8_ideal.json` and `ideal_eight_range_state_encoding_bound` in the architecture report.

### Physical first-boundary charge-switch screen — 2026-09-30

Built a TT/27°C physical MIM/TG differential bench with a10µm fine capacitor and13.3µm extension capacitor per side (model estimates207.6fF and363.9fF). A 112.5mV differential prepared on both banks nearly retains its intended value after the physical connection:112.4997mV. The fine pair reads113.1975mV immediately before connection because the prior initialization TG release contributed~0.698mV; the connection removes that preparation offset. Thus the raw−0.698mV before/after difference is **not** an isolated range-switch kick. If the extension is prepared only at VCM, the connection leaves41.024mV, consistent with the predicted0.363 charge-share factor; it cannot preserve a continuous piecewise state. Matched extension preparation is performed by *ideal voltage sources*, with no actual tracking/precharge circuit.

With matched ideal preparation, a signed +100nA/−100nA50ns current packet applied after connection gives17.3545mV event-minus-reference step versus17.4982mV from the simple capacitance estimate (−0.82%). In a separate manually timed crossing experiment, the fine state starts at100mV, the current pulse starts, and the extension connects13ns into that pulse at the modeled112.5mV boundary. The measured immediate final state is125.296mV versus125.473mV for an ideal two-capacitance piecewise integration (−0.177mV). That is a deterministic, ideal-current positive-crossing result, **not** a range detector or an actual float-weight MOS event. The connection measurement spans ongoing event current and should not be interpreted as a pure switch-injection number.

The 5ms continuation is a failure for uncorrected storage: the matched branch's event-minus-reference differential falls from17.3543mV just after the packet to16.1706mV at5.1ms, a1.1837mV/6.82% loss; the unexcited branch's stored112.499mV differential also falls to104.826mV. The VCM-precharge control has essentially the same event-difference decay. This nominal PDK leakage/hold screen omits thermal noise, mismatch, threshold/beta/subtractive reset, feedback, a real precharge driver, bidirectional boundary switching, actual mapped weights, 30.976m state inventory, and extracted layout. Therefore the eight-range map remains an **ideal replay bound**. The next physical gate is boundary-reference programming plus bidirectional switching, then retention compensation without changing the float state semantics. Evidence: `scripts/test_sky130_piecewise_range_switch.py`, the six base `sky130_piecewise_range_switch_*.json` reports, and `physical_piecewise_range_switch_screen` in the architecture boundary report.

The extension need not track an arbitrary state if it is connected *exactly at a known segment boundary*: its target is a fixed per-segment reference voltage. That may allow a shared analog reference and local TG programming, although reference distribution, timing and per-cell storage remain untested. To bound its accuracy, shifted only the extension's ideal differential reference during the manually timed positive crossing. A +0.1mV precharge error moves final state by+0.0635mV relative to zero-offset; +1/−1mV errors move it by+0.6354/−0.6353mV. This nearly matches the extension's63.67% share of total capacitance. These are deterministic voltage stresses, not mismatch yield. The next bench should replace the ideal boundary source with a physical shared reference/programming path and measure timing/error under signed crossings; uncorrected5ms hold remains a separate blocker.

The **full eight-range per-state capacitor-bank implementation is rejected on area**. If event charge per model unit stays fixed and only switched local capacitance changes the voltage slope, the final segment's4,495.9× lower slope needs about933pF per side from a207.6fF fine side. The local SKY130 typical MIM model gives a square side about683µm for that capacitance; two such sides for every30,976,000 logical states imply≈28.9m² bare MIM area, before switches, routing and references. This is a conditional architecture bound, not a layout measurement or a universal lower bound on analog storage. It rules out scaling the tested10µm+13.3µm *per-state cap extension mechanism* through the full slope span. The software eight-range/`asinh` results remain upper bounds; a viable analog option would need state-dependent event-charge scaling on a modest store, shared active circuitry, or another physical representation. The measured direct-MOS bank has not demonstrated that state-dependent charge law.

### Actual float-weight demand on the eight-range update — 2026-09-30

Reconstructed the exact signed float-weight multisets and multiplicities from the three saved final-fusion physical-deck sites and replayed their current-bin sums from zero through the ideal eight-range voltage map. The near-threshold329-event site requests at most0.858mV per event and the322-event high-cancellation site at most1.220mV, independent of forward/reverse/shuffled order within their first segment. All those requests are below the bank-loaded SS/0°C calibrated16.624mV one-event step. The370-event maximum-fan-in site spans multiple ranges: largest requested step is29.9mV forward,341.9mV reverse and245.4mV shuffled. Respectively5,16 and22 events exceed that measured one-event step; an optimistic equal-ceiling split needs at least5,74 and58 additional event slots. This arithmetic is not a circuit schedule or evidence that the bank can deliver those steps at the stored common-mode/state voltage. The final encoded state is−617.35mV for all orders, while intermediate paths and crossing counts differ. Prior state, beta, threshold, physical state-dependent control, noise, hold and feedback are omitted. Evidence: `scripts/analyze_sky130_piecewise_actual_event_range.py`, `sky130_piecewise_actual_event_range_three_sites.json` and `actual_float_weight_piecewise_event_demand` in the architecture boundary report. The existing linear bank cannot be promoted to an eight-range injector from its present calibration.
At the source deck's50.253ns nominal slot, those optimistic max-fanin subdivisions occupy18.84/22.31/21.51µs in forward/reverse/shuffled order within a5ms bin. This only bounds one isolated site's source occupancy; it omits state sensing, gain switching, reference settling, transfer nonlinearity and sharing across30,976,000 logical membranes.
None of the three reconstructed sites' ideal encoded steps falls below the source deck's measured0.350µV single-event floor in these orders. That is a local demand result, not full-network coverage.

A bounded follow-up actually precharged a10µm MIM pair to differential−1.2/0/+0.6/+1.2V and pulsed either sign of the geometry-4 branch of the shared13-geometry MOS bank at42.2657ns, gate fraction0.95, SS/0°C. Eight physical first-event cases with matched no-event controls give absolute event steps16.209–17.701mV, a9.20% max/min spread. The maximum-drive source is broadly similar in magnitude across these prepared states; its passive dependence does not supply the~4,496× segment slope span or a precise state-dependent `f(m+Δm)−f(m)` law. The sweep does not show whether a programmed set of smaller, repeated pulses can track that law with charge conservation, signed cancellation and acceptable service time. Evidence: `scripts/analyze_sky130_ms_state_bias_step.py`, `sky130_ms_state_bias_step_ss0_geom4.json` and `physical_MOS_event_stored_state_sweep` in the architecture report.

Four more physical cases used the smallest geometry0.5 branch at gate fraction0.9 with precharged−0.6/+0.6V differential and both signs. Matched-control event magnitudes are1.3115–1.3377mV, a2.00% max/min span, near the high-cancellation site's largest1.220mV ideal request. This is only one fixed pulse setting; an actual weight/state mapper would need a programmed continuous transfer and precision/noise characterization. None of these twelve first-event cases exercises repeated pulses, range crossings or the full5ms hold.

Extended the sequence bench to prepare a nonzero differential and keep the full13-geometry MOS bank connected with inactive branches off. Its10ns TG release lead and2ns gate slew now match the first-event calibration. At zero initial differential, a geometry0.5/gate0.9 event contributes±1.326062mV and ten same-sign pulses contribute±13.26091mV; five positive/five negative alternating pulses leave only±0.000042mV relative to a matched no-event branch. At−0.6V, individual positive/negative steps are+1.3383/−1.3120mV, a26.3µV magnitude imbalance; alternating ten pulses leave+0.1310/+0.1312mV across the two orders. At+0.6V the corresponding residuals are−0.1312/−0.1310mV. The residual is systematic in this nominal schematic, though still smaller than the independent-pair~192µV RMS reset-noise proxy. The model's separate high-cancellation site's final ideal encoded current contribution is only5.44µV, but these equal-pulse tests are **not** that site's weighted event sequence, so the figures cannot be directly subtracted as a prediction. A state-aware signed source or correction must preserve cancellation across stored-state bias before full-float fan-in is credible. No physical state sensing, programmed correction, noise/mismatch, five-ms hold or feedback has been demonstrated. Evidence: `scripts/test_sky130_ms_first_touch_long_event_sequence.py`, the `_lead10n_*_rise2n_banked.json` reports and `physical_MOS_event_stored_state_sweep` in the architecture boundary report.

The same bank-loaded ten-pulse deck now accepts independent positive and negative analog gate fractions. At−0.6V, raising only the weaker negative fraction from0.90000 to0.90100 reduces the largest alternating residual131.2→18.5µV;0.90116 reduces it to0.5µV. At+0.6V, raising only the weaker positive fraction to0.90116 reduces131.2→0.6µV. The stronger fixed-sign gate stays0.90000. Thus a small **manually selected** polarity correction can restore deterministic cancellation at these two known states, with~219–262× reduction in the maximum residual. This is a conditional existence screen, not a physical state sensor, adaptive controller, R-2R resolution/noise result, weight-accurate event sequence, or retention proof. The correction curve over intermediate states and actual mixed float weights remains open.

An intermediate-state check used a half-size correction: at−0.3V the uncorrected maximum alternating residual is65.9µV and gate fraction0.90058 on the weaker negative polarity reduces it to0.4µV; at+0.3V the residual is65.4µV and the same0.90058 correction on the weaker positive polarity reduces it to0.3µV. Together with±0.6V, these four nominal points support an approximately proportional **manual** bias law, `weaker_fraction≈0.90000+0.00193·|prepared_differential_V|` for this one geometry, pulse width, corner and temperature. It is an interpolation target, not an implemented state-feedback circuit or a PVT/mismatch law.

### Actual float fan-in within the first analog range — 2026-10-01

Added a guarded first-segment mode to the existing original-float event exporter. It uses the ideal eight-range map's first slope,0.0473741V per model unit, only if every intermediate event sum stays within±2.374716 model units. The329-event near-threshold and322-event high-cancellation sites both satisfy that guard in forward/reverse/shuffled orders. The calibrated, shared13-geometry SKY130 SS/0°C MOS bank,10µm differential MIM, physical initialization TG and ideal effective gate PWL commands then carry the full signed event multiset; no QAT or quantized reference is used.

For the near-threshold site at50.253ns per event, the ideal sum is23,699.118µV. Physical sums are23,741.490µV forward,23,668.560µV reverse and23,709.870µV shuffled: errors+42.372/−30.558/+10.752µV, or+0.179/−0.129/+0.045% of the ideal magnitude. The order spread is72.93µV. A separate shuffle-only deck gave23,711.260µV, showing a1.39µV deck-composition difference; use the three-order deck for the matched comparison. The model's float candidate-to-threshold margin would scale to~−2.97mV under this ideal first-segment representation, but this experiment has **no** physical threshold, beta/prior membrane, readout or noise and cannot claim a correct neuron decision.

The high-cancellation site has an ideal signed sum of only+5.442µV. Its50.253ns four-branch deck failed ngspice timestep convergence near10.14µs; no final result was taken from it. At a wider55ns slot, the completed physical sums are+17.066µV forward,−61.647µV reverse and−17.553µV shuffled, with errors+11.624/−67.089/−22.995µV and78.713µV order spread. The shuffled sum reverses sign. Thus first-range voltage gain alone does not deliver accurate cancellation, even though all requested individual event steps fall inside the measured one-event range.

Applying the equal-pulse `0.00193` gate-fraction-per-volt polarity adjustment as an **ideal event-by-event feedforward estimate** to the actual mixed weights reduces high-cancellation spread to46.013µV and improves forward/reverse errors to−4.570/−50.583µV, but shuffled error worsens to−24.849µV. The one-geometry correction coefficient is not a general weight/geometry calibration. No physical state sensor, bias programmer, R-2R ladder, eight-range crossing, five-ms hold, noise/mismatch, full PLIF feedback or layout is included. Evidence: `scripts/analyze_sky130_piecewise_first_range_physical.py`, `sky130_piecewise_first_range_physical_boundary.json` and `physical_piecewise_first_segment_float_fanin` in the architecture boundary report. Full analog readiness stays false.

The physical mixed-weight deck uses all13 geometries, with125/322 events on geometry4; effective ideal gate-fraction percentiles are0.428/0.669/0.677/0.715/0.782 at0/10/50/90/100%. Therefore the equal-pulse geometry0.5/fraction0.9 correction was outside most of this distribution. At a representative geometry4/fraction0.67683, a physical one-event screen precharged to−/+30mV gives positive steps206.51/205.84µV and mirrored negative steps−205.84/−206.51µV, a0.67µV sign imbalance at either state. In a separate55ns shuffle-only mixed-weight deck, prefix measurements relative to a no-event branch show signed error+13.49µV after40 events,−0.91µV after80,−20.58µV after120, then roughly−18 to−26µV through322. The final near-zero sum exposes accumulated earlier error rather than a singular last-event failure. Prefix samples occur5ns after a pulse and may retain settlement; the all-order deck has a~1µV composition difference at the final point. Evidence: `scripts/analyze_sky130_piecewise_prefix_physical.py` and `sky130_piecewise_first_range_highcancel_prefix40.json`.

### Bounded MOS gate-capacitor state-store screen — 2026-10-01

`scripts/screen_sky130_mos_state_cap.py` ran three SKY130 TT/nominal Docker ngspice decks with equal **100µm² drawn capacitor area**: ten `nfet_01v8` gates of10µm×1µm with drain/source/body at ground, and one10µm×10µm MIM. A physical transmission gate samples0.6/0.9/1.2V; an ideal2fC current pulse measures local incremental capacitance against a no-pulse branch; both branches hold to5ms. All three decks completed. The gate gives0.650/0.770/0.797pF versus MIM0.207pF, a3.14–3.84× capacitance ratio that varies with state voltage. The no-pulse MOS drift from~2µs to5ms is+4.568/+0.0017/−3.726mV, versus MIM+14.165/+0.023/−14.112mV. Thus the MOS gate has a real nominal density advantage at this bias, but the sampled off-center states still move by millivolts across the bin. The independent-pair kT/C arithmetic at300K remains102–113µV for the measured MOS cap, far above the few-µV cancellation/readout scale. This is a noise estimate, not simulated transient noise.

At equal *capacitance* to the earlier10µm MIM pair, scaling the measured gate-only area across30,976,000 states gives an optimistic1,613–1,975mm² bare-gate range, before diffusion, contacts, switches, routing, references or layout rules. It is therefore not an area-qualified macro. These benches use a transistor gate as an experimental capacitor, not a qualified PDK MOSCAP cell, and omit mismatch, extraction, refresh, differential correlation and physical readout. Do not promote this result to an accepted analog state store or apply its TT behavior to SS/FF. Evidence: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_mos_state_cap_boundary.json` and `physical_MOS_gate_state_cap_screen` in the architecture boundary report.

The conditional refresh-service calculation takes the measured0.6/1.2V drift as a one-pole pull toward0.9V. Under an **illustrative**3µV allowable drift at a0.3V stored offset, it implies a3.26–4.00µs refresh interval. Across30,976,000 states, even an idealized1µs visit would need7.74–9.51million parallel channels. At100µV allowed drift, the same arithmetic still needs232k–285k channels. These are channel-count lower bounds under a model fitted to one5ms nominal result, not measured refresh timing, model accuracy limits, or a circuit design. Refreshing by copying a sensed value also cannot reconstruct a previous state after unknown noise or leakage. Evidence: `scripts/analyze_sky130_mos_refresh_service.py`, `sky130_mos_refresh_service_boundary.json`.

A five-voltage physical-TG hold screen checked whether one calibrated exponential law could correct the nominal leakage at the normal update boundary. Fitting the0.6V trace at2µs and5ms predicts its own intermediate times within3.22µV; reusing that fit at0.75V misses the5ms state by−258.41µV. The1.2V fit predicts its own trace within0.73µV, but misses1.05V by−21.78µV at5ms. This shows voltage dependence even in the selected TT model; it does not rule out a more detailed physical calibration. No sensor or correction circuit was instantiated, and the readout/noise and signed-event gates remain open. Evidence: `scripts/screen_sky130_mos_decay_prediction.py`, `sky130_mos_decay_prediction_boundary.json`.

The next physical isolation screen placed two TGs in series and varied a held MIM capacitor on their middle node from none to a10µm square. At0.6V, all stacked cases drift+4.569 to+4.575mV over5ms versus+4.568mV for one TG; at1.2V, stacked drift is−3.727 to−3.732mV versus−3.726mV. The middle node itself moves by tens to hundreds of millivolts, so its capacitor does not preserve the state. A diagnostic ideal-initial-condition run isolates the leakage source: bare MOS gate storage has zero nominal drift; an off NFET alone pulls the0.6/1.2V state down4.667/7.535mV, and an off PFET alone pulls it up9.199/3.789mV. Both off in parallel give+4.560/−3.740mV. Thus these PDK transistor branches, including the device directly attached to the state node, dominate the sampled hold; adding a second series TG is not an effective repair. The ideal `.ic` diagnostic is not a physical programming result, and none of these cases includes noise or layout. Evidence: `scripts/screen_sky130_mos_stacked_isolation.py`, `scripts/screen_sky130_mos_switch_leakage.py`, `sky130_mos_stacked_isolation_boundary.json`, `sky130_mos_switch_leakage_boundary.json`.

Schematic body-to-state variants in the same off-switch diagnostic did not qualify a repair. At0.6V, the NFET-only variant drifts+2.356mV; at1.2V the PFET-only variant drifts−1.939mV. The0.6V PFET body-tracking case places its body0.3V below its source, **outside** SkyWater's documented−0.1V lower (V_{BS}) limit, so its+83.767mV result and the corresponding combined-switch result cannot be used for device selection. The1.2V NFET body-tracking case starts at the documented+0.3V (V_{BS}) edge; its−92.728mV result is a boundary diagnostic only. None has isolated-well layout or physical initialization. The [SkyWater device documentation](https://skywater-pdk.readthedocs.io/en/main/rules/device-details.html) also says the special SRAM pass devices are restricted to hard-IP SRAM arrays; they are not a custom analog-state switch option. A different supported switch model or process would need its own retention, noise and layout qualification.

An official public-model extension then compared `nfet_g5v0d10v5`/`pfet_g5v0d10v5` at1µm/0.5µm against the installed1.8V devices at1µm/0.15µm, using the same100µm² MOS gate state,1.8V controls and10µs physical-TG programming. Both0.6/1.2V TT decks completed. The5V pair programmed within−0.759/−0.086mV and drifted+4.553/−3.715mV by5ms; the1.8V pair drifted+4.587/−3.714mV. The thicker-device substitution therefore provides no material retention improvement in this comparison. The public model files were added from the official [SkyWater primitive repository](https://github.com/google/skywater-pdk-libs-sky130_fd_pr); exact revision equivalence to the installed subset, legal analog layout, noise, mismatch, other corners and service remain unverified. Evidence: `scripts/screen_sky130_hv_switch_state.py`, `sky130_hv_switch_state_boundary.json`.

`scripts/screen_sky130_mos_fullrail_hold.py` physically sampled the100µm² MOS gate bank through the1.8V TG at13 programmed levels from0 to1.8V and measured the held gate at2µs through5ms. From0.15 through1.8V,5ms drift varies nonlinearly: +28.639mV at0.15V,+4.568mV at0.6V,near zero at0.9V,−3.726mV at1.2V and−9.140mV at1.65V. Programming errors are separate and reach roughly1.8mV over the sampled rail. The selected SS/0°C, TT/27°C and FF/85°C hold decks at0.15/0.9/1.65V all complete; at1.65V their drift is−9.290/−9.140/−2.203mV. These are three coupled process/temperature points, not a full corner matrix, and include no mismatch, switched noise or extraction. Evidence: `sky130_mos_fullrail_hold_boundary.json` and `sky130_mos_pvt_hold_boundary.json`.

The original epoch58 full-float checkpoint was replayed across all25 PLIF actors on two complete DSEC validation frames. At each update after the first, its ideal voltage-encoded stored state receives interpolated physical TT5ms MOS drift only within measured voltages; states outside that window remain ideal. The initial0.6–1.2V window covers only33.1/32.5% of state accesses and yields1.25/1.28% final-fusion spike mismatch. The expanded0.148–1.801V window covers98.6% on both frames; deterministic drift yields1.92/1.93% spike mismatch and90.0/90.1% pixel agreement with float. A three-seed illustrative **113µV RMS independent differential reset-noise proxy** plus drift yields mean2.91/2.94% spike mismatch,85.7/85.2% pixel agreement, and mean present-class IoU0.2073/0.2017 versus float0.2057/0.1989. Noise alone gives2.13/2.13% spike mismatch. A zero-noise, zero-drift control exactly reproduces float spikes and pixel classes. These two frames do not establish dataset accuracy; near-baseline IoU can coexist with extensive changed decisions. The113µV figure is a kT/C-scale proxy for the measured small MOS cap, not physical switched readout noise. The piecewise encoding, state-dependent signed update/inverse, calibration and programming path remain ideal. Evidence: `scripts/analyze_sky130_float_state_saturation_replay.py`, `scripts/analyze_sky130_mos_retention_float_boundary.py`, `sky130_mos_retention_float_boundary.json`.

The simpler per-record-normalized **linear** state map uses the same physical drift and113µV proxy. Its deterministic drift alone yields1.16/1.15% final-fusion spike mismatch, but three-seed drift-plus-noise yields13.82/13.66% mismatch and mean present-class IoU0.1192/0.1185 versus float0.2057/0.1989. Noise-only gives nearly the same loss. Under this independent reset-noise assumption, the small MOS linear state cell fails the two-frame task sensitivity screen. This does not exclude a physically demonstrated correlated or first-touch noise mechanism; neither is implemented in the cell. Full physical piecewise encoding remains a research target, not a macro ready for layout.

### Matched-load MOS injector replay and state dependence — 2026-10-01

The first MOS injector replay used a calibration map with only the selected geometry attached; review showed this did not match the fan-in deck, where all13 geometries remain electrically connected. Treat that first replay as preliminary. `scripts/characterize_sky130_ms_mos_state_banked_transfer.py` measured104 positive geometry/fraction points with the complete injector bank loaded and two physical100µm² MOS gate-cap banks at SS/0°C; the negative transfer is mirrored from the separate polarity symmetry check. `scripts/test_sky130_ms_mos_state_injector.py` then replayed the original epoch58 full-float322-event high-cancellation site using the matched-load map. Against the+5.442µV ideal target, forward/reverse/shuffle final outputs were+27.076/−9.900/+10.572µV, with final errors+21.634/−15.342/+5.130µV. Maximum sampled prefix errors were0.159/0.157/0.0279mV; reverse order has two sampled prefix sign mismatches and ends with the wrong sign. All controls and geometry selections remain ideal, and this is one output site and one SS/0°C condition.

`scripts/characterize_sky130_ms_mos_state_dependence.py` completed18 full-bank cases at±31mV differential and0.9V common mode, geometry4, gate fractions0.8/0.85/0.9, both polarities. Measured event magnitude changes by less than about1% across that tested state span; polarity asymmetry is small. No physical bias DAC, state sensor/feedback, noise/mismatch, 5ms retention during the train, threshold/reset/beta, extraction, layout or population service is established. ### Ideal state-bias compensation spot sweep — 2026-10-01

The matched-load geometry4 transfer sweep found a small polarity/state dependence around a0.9V common mode. An exploratory `--ideal-state-polarity-correction` applies a same-direction-only gate-fraction increment proportional to ideal accumulated state; slope is recorded per report. On the322-event high-cancellation site, slope0.0022/V changes forward/reverse/shuffle endpoint errors to+3.794/+3.317/+2.996µV against the+5.442µV ideal target, removes sampled sign mismatches, and leaves maximum sampled prefix errors0.176/0.175/0.0293mV. The state value is supplied by the exporter’s ideal accumulator; there is no physical sensing or correction circuit.

The same setting on a separate329-event near-threshold-scale trace gives+0.164/+0.156/+0.160mV endpoint error against+23.699mV, with maximum sampled prefix error0.164/0.253/0.160mV. Separate no-correction forward/reverse runs give+0.160/+0.124mV error, so this two-point calibrated correction does not generalize. The no-correction all-order run fails transient convergence during shuffle at12.79µs; its final values are unavailable. These traces show that one state-correction slope cannot yet be accepted as a general rule. Calibration remains at SS/0°C and one high-use geometry/bias range; physical sensor, OTA/driver, noisy/mismatched devices, complete PLIF dynamics, layout/extraction and service are still absent. Evidence: `sky130_ms_mos_state_injector_boundary_statecorr_0p0022.json`, `sky130_ms_mos_state_injector_boundary_near_threshold_scale_statecorr_0p0022.json`, and no-correction order reports under `artifacts/analog_asic/results/`.

### SKY130 Magic PCell unit probe — 2026-10-01

Installed Magic 8.3.306 against Ciel SKY130A v1.0.335. A first MOS PCell GDS probe exposed an unsafe unit assumption: the generator passes `w` and `l` through `magic::spice2float` and then directly to its geometry generator, while the generated extraction reported extreme dimensions for bare numeric inputs and minimum-device clamping for suffixed `u` inputs. The resulting `cap100.gds` and `sky130_mos_cap_10x10u.gds` are diagnostic artifacts only; neither is a valid state-capacitor layout. DRC reporting at the top wrapper is not sufficient because the hierarchy and electrical terminal mapping have not been validated. Do not reuse either GDS. Next use a controlled parameter sweep with child-cell geometry bounds, per-cell DRC, and extracted terminal dimensions, then implement connected plates and rerun DRC/LVS/extraction. No analog macro layout is complete.
**Correction to the earlier layout note (2026-10-01):** The PCell input units are bare micron values, matching its documented defaults (`w=0.42`, `l=0.15`); the suffixed `u` values caused the clamp. A three-case sweep (minimum device, doubled width, ten fingers) generated GDS with the expected width scaling and reports zero geometry DRC errors. The 10µm-per-finger candidate GDS is about14.46×11.99µm. The prior claim that bare values imply extreme geometry was based on misreading ext2spice parameter formatting and is withdrawn. Extraction is still not validated: PCell subterminals are unconnected in the probe top, Magic reported extraction feedback errors, and a standard-scale equivalence transient/DC check is still needed. Use `ext2spice scale on` (which emits integer internal dimensions plus `.option scale=5000u`) for the comparison, and save the complete hierarchy with `writeall force`; `save` alone writes only the top cell. These remain diagnostic PCell tests, not a qualified capacitor macro or full layout.

### Differential MOS state-sensor first screen — 2026-10-01

`scripts/characterize_sky130_differential_state_sensor.py` sweeps a symmetric NMOS differential pair with PMOS mirror load at TT/27°C and0.9V input common mode. The ideal-schematic zero-input mismatch is effectively zero. Differential output changes by about±36.28µV for±3µV input and±72.55µV for±6µV, giving12.092V/V local gain; the±30µV slope is the same. Output common level is about0.662V and tail node0.221V. This establishes a nominal open-loop DC transfer only; it says nothing about mismatch, noise, latch decision margin, startup, speed or layout.

`scripts/test_sky130_differential_sensor_state_loading.py` then attaches the pair's gate inputs to two extracted21.837pF M3_2 banks initialized to a30µV differential, and compares against a matched uninstrumented pair. Over5ms, sensor-pair differential changes−13.67nV; the reference changes−13.73nV, so excess disturbance is about+0.06nV. The output is0.3628mV differential at2µs and0.3626mV at5ms. This indicates negligible loading for a very large MIM bank in the nominal model. The bench contains four capacitor-bank instances (one sensor pair and one reference pair), ideal programmed initial values, schematic TG switches and an open-loop sensor. It has no noise, mismatch, physical latch/bias loop, compact-cap test or layout. Next quantify integrated input-referred noise and settling, include statistical mismatch if the model supports it, and repeat with smaller storage before laying out this sensor.

The same DC topology has local gain8.608V/V at SS/0°C and15.501V/V at FF/85°C. A deliberately altered input-device width gives a first-order structural sensitivity check: widening one of the two NMOS devices by1% shifts the inferred input crossing by about0.678mV; a5% perturbation shifts it about3.303mV. This is a deterministic geometry perturbation and not a process mismatch distribution. The PDK's mismatch annotations in this model set are Spectre `vary` statements; this ngspice setup does not draw statistical device variations. Consequently the ideal zero offset under TT symmetry is not a realistic offset qualification. The untrimmed sensor has no credible few-microvolt margin; compare autozero/trim and regenerative readout with correlated reset before selecting a topology. Evidence: `sky130_diff_sensor_dc_ss_temp0_mismatch0pct.json` and `sky130_diff_sensor_dc_ff_temp85_mismatch0pct.json` plus mismatch perturbation JSONs under `artifacts/analog_asic/results/`.

Open-loop noise integration from10Hz to1MHz at TT/27°C reports58.61µV RMS input-referred; the limited10Hz–10kHz band reports32.92µV and10Hz–1kHz reports24.55µV. The10Hz–1kHz screen was repeated after parallelizing input pairs and PMOS mirror loads: ten devices per side reduce this to8.92µV; one hundred reduce it to2.985µV. Attempting to set PDK `nf` to10/100 causes BSIM parameter-check failures in ngspice; those failed runs are retained as invalid, and the successful scaling uses explicit parallel instances of the supported10µm×1µm devices. The 100-pair/100-load topology contains about6,000µm² nominal channel geometry per logical state (2,000µm² input pair plus4,000µm² PMOS loads); multiplying by30.976m states is≈0.186m² before routing. The measured MIM bank's33.62×378µm bbox adds≈0.394m² over the inventory, for a rough combined lower-bound estimate≈0.58m². This ignores routing, wells, power, latch, update and support circuitry, and the sensor noise still only narrowly meets the sampled few-microvolt margin under a 1kHz bandwidth. The noise calculation is small-signal and is not switched-noise or statistical mismatch evidence. A shared/auto-zeroed regenerative front end must now be explored; the plain per-state diff pair is not selected.

### Differential sensor noise/area screen — 2026-10-01

The SKY130 open-loop differential sensor noise bench was extended to three device scales. ### Full-frame reset-noise envelope — 2026-10-01

Used the original epoch-58 full-float checkpoint with QAT disabled and replayed two complete DSEC validation frames through all25 PLIF actors. The existing zero-noise control reproduces float predictions exactly. Independent Gaussian differential state perturbation applied only after subtractive spike resets gives the following three-seed means: at6µV RMS, mean IoU is0.2031 vs0.2057 on record0 and0.1982 vs0.1989 on record1; labeled pixel accuracy is0.4923 vs0.4982 and0.4824 vs0.4818. Final-fusion spike mismatch remains≈4.4% and≈4.8%, so this is approximate task-metric robustness, not spike-equivalent inference. At20.5µV RMS (the estimated independent-pair kT/C proxy for the100µm MIM state cap), mean IoU falls to0.1192 and0.1178 from0.2057 and0.1989; labeled accuracy falls to0.3562 and0.3505 from0.4982 and0.4818. Thus direct use of that reset-noise scale fails the two-frame accuracy screen. Intermediate sensitivities on record0 are stored at0.01/0.1/0.3/3µV as well.

This is a software sensitivity model: noise is added independently to membrane state after spike reset, while event currents, analog threshold, beta, injector, retention and update timing remain ideal. It does not measure actual circuit reset noise. The result establishes a provisional state/reset noise target of≤6µV RMS for follow-up, not a qualified circuit bound. Area-scaling a two-capacitor100µm MIM pair from20.5µV to6µV by kT/C alone needs11.67× capacitance, about0.233mm² per state pair and≈7.23m² of bare capacitor over30.976m states. This makes passive cap enlargement an unattractive fix. Next physically characterize the reset/threshold switch's noise and seek correlated or charge-domain cancellation; then inject measured residual noise into the same full-float replay before selecting any PLIF layout. Evidence: `sky130_float_state_saturation_validation_{0,1}_all_neurons_full_reset_only_g1_n{0,6,20.5}uv.json` and record0 `n{0.01,0.1,0.3,3}uv.json`.

Neither fixed size corrects the false-spike decision; the sampled latch input remains approximately−8µV. An ideal−10µV threshold adjustment restores this selected no-spike decision, with0.082µV bucket error and0.031µV read disturbance. These simulations isolate a threshold-readout offset problem and provide one trim-range point. They do not implement a physical trim DAC/calibration loop and contain no mismatch distribution, transient noise or extracted layout. Evidence: `sky130_ss_complementary_latch_pair_size_sweep.json` and `physical_active_bucket_complementary_input_latch_size_sweep` in `sky130_readout_architecture_boundary.json`.
### Readout range and topology screen — 2026-10-02

A standalone SKY130 TT transient sweep maps the complementary NMOS/PMOS clocked latch polarity: it reads both signs correctly through an input differential of about60mV, then gives reversed decisions by about120mV. The actual max-fanin input is−211.85mV. A 4:1 attenuator around VCM brings that case to−54.4mV and restores the expected polarity in the isolated test.

On the complete370-event max-fanin OTA/MIM replay, removing the auxiliary PMOS input pair yields the correct no-spike decision at TT (event error+4.03µV) and FF (event error+2.83mV). The FF increment is inaccurate despite its correct threshold decision; SS transient fails numerically at3.46ms (`vthbias#branch`, timestep6.25e−21s), so no SS circuit result is available. Existing 329-event direct NMOS-only near-threshold runs pass TT but fail SS and FF due untrimmed readout offset.

The 4:1 switched attenuator preserves the max-fanin TT loaded state to within0.003µV, matches the event update within58µV and gives the correct no-spike decision. It also passes the near-threshold TT case (update error−0.705µV, correct no-spike), but fails the same near-threshold FF case (false spike at−3.56µV sampled differential); high-cancellation TT also false-spikes. Its SS transient hits convergence trouble at `vthbias#branch`. Fixed attenuation is therefore rejected as a complete readout path. The experimental clocked PMOS preamp is also rejected as misbiased: its tail stayed near1.798V and output common mode below0.5mV, so its apparently resolving latch outputs are not meaningful.

Use the NMOS-only latch as the current wide-range lead and investigate a physical analog autozero/trim that preserves near-zero gain. Layout remains gated pending signed decisions over PVT/mismatch/noise, accurate state transfer, low read disturbance, state retention and 5ms service. Reports: `sky130_regenerative_latch_input_range.json`, `sky130_regenerative_latch_input_range_attenuated.json`, `sky130_sc_integrator_diffpair_max_fanin_full_shuffle_{tt,ff}_latch*.json`, `sky130_sc_integrator_diffpair_near_threshold_scale_full_shuffle_{tt,ff}_latch*.json`, `sky130_sc_integrator_diffpair_high_cancellation_full_shuffle_tt_latch*.json`, and `sky130_monotonic_readout_preamplifier.json`.
Follow-up NMOS-only latch replay at the322-event high-cancellation site still false-spikes: the mapped float margin is−5.77µV and the sampled latch differential is+125.7µV. This is consistent with the uncalibrated offset dominating the smallest margins, despite the wide-range max-fanin improvement. An SS max-fanin rerun with a1MΩ series resistor on the ideal threshold source and another with Gear integration both fail at `vthbias#branch` before the decision phase; neither numerical change resolves the convergence issue.
### Sampled autozero thermal-noise area bound — 2026-10-02

Used the extracted SKY130 10×10µm MIM result (206.582fF over100µm², 2.066fF/µm²) and the epoch58 float trace's exploratory mapped q0.1 absolute margin (0.5373µV) to bound independent two-cap sampled-offset correction. At300K, keeping differential kT/C below0.5373µV requires28.70nF per leg, about13.89mm² per leg and27.78mm² per state identity. Replicated over30,976,000 membrane identities, bare MIM alone would occupy about860.7m². At a looser6µV differential-noise comparison, the pair still costs0.223mm² per state or6.90m² total. A measured10×10µm MIM pair's analytic differential kT/C is about200µV.

This is an optimistic thermal-noise lower bound: it excludes switches, parasitics, calibration resolution, mismatch and routing. The0.5373µV quantile comes from sampled float records and is a conservative comparison point, not a selected error-rate requirement. The calculation rules out independent per-identity MIM autozero storage at these scales; it motivates shared readout or a non-sampled offset-cancellation method. It does not change the full-float analog objective. Reproduce with `scripts/analyze_sky130_autozero_noise_area.py`; machine report: `sky130_autozero_noise_area_boundary.json`.

The same report also derives a shared-readout throughput lower bound:30,976,000 state decisions every5ms is6.1952G decisions/s. At the observed2.6µs ideal-trim latch evaluation time, at least16,108 comparators must run in parallel even before mux, calibration, reset, event and graph overhead. Multiplexed comparison is therefore possible only with substantial parallelism or a much faster analog readout; it is not yet a selected architecture.

# Shared OTA common-mode controller polarity screen — 2026-10-02

The shared differential OTA-C bucket's output PMOS gate has a strong, monotonic DC control range in TT/27°C, SS/0°C and FF/85°C: a fixed-gate sweep from 0 to 1.8V takes the equal outputs from about 1.799V toward 0V. A separate controller OTA DC sweep identifies its sign. When the sensed mean rises above 0.9V, connecting that sense to the controller's `inn` input drives its output gate high, which reduces PMOS pull-up and corrects the output downward. Connecting the mean to `inp` drives the gate low and is positive feedback. The existing normal wiring (`vcm` to `inp`, mean to `inn`) therefore has the correct sign; reversing it was wrong.

This resolves polarity but not the closed-loop problem. Existing normal-polarity transients oscillate/drift across the 5ms screen, while reversed wiring rails high. The DC transfer is steep near the 0.9V crossing and has essentially rail-level output immediately on either side, so this OTA at its current bias/sizing is a poor linear controller around the target. Do not lay out or accept this CMFB candidate. Next test a lower-gain/controlled-bandwidth controller and explicit operating-point strategy, then rerun the zero-event hold across corners before injecting events. This result says nothing about full-float state fidelity, noise, mismatch, layout, or full-model operation.

Reproduction: `python scripts/screen_sky130_cmfb_ota_polarity.py`; machine-readable report and decks/logs are under `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_cmfb_ota_polarity/`. The output gate range sweep is under `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_diff_ota_gate_transfer/`.

A low-complexity passive alternative (two20MΩ output-to-common-gate paths and a1MΩ gate-bias resistor) is stable but unsuitable as the state cell. With a0.9V reference its5ms common mode is0.113V TT,0.029V SS and1.011V FF. Fine trim sweeps show steep, process-dependent transitions: at0.812V reference TT gives0.907V, while SS gives0.461V and FF gives1.728V. Starting with1mV differential state at0.812V, TT differential falls below0.105mV by100µs and to zero at1ms; feedback resistors couple differential state into the common gate and erase it. Reject this passive topology for membrane storage. The low-gain OTA variants (`win=1um`, `wn3=0.5um`, `cc=50pF`, and the `wn3=0.5um` case) also rail or wander and are rejected. The CMFB script supports `--passive-cmfb`, `--biasref=...` and `--diffinit=...`; JSON and decks/logs are in matching `sky130_shared_diff_ota_cmfb_*` folders.

Source degeneration (100Ω–10kΩ in each CMFB input leg) with larger compensation (50–100pF) was screened to reduce the OTA's effective gain. The most promising sampled point was100Ω,100pF, and0.45V CMFB tail bias: TT/27°C ends at0.927V, but SS/0°C collapses to0.0003V and FF/85°C ends at0.662V. Other degeneration values and the original0.65V tail bias wander or rail. This does not pass PVT and is not a candidate for layout. The degenerated subcircuit is generated in `scripts/screen_sky130_shared_diff_ota_cmfb.py` and the parameterized reports reside under `artifacts/analog_asic/results/`.

## Direct differential switched-capacitor membrane cell — 2026-10-02

Built `scripts/test_sky130_differential_cap_packet.py` for two physical-model MIM storage capacitors, two matched MIM packet capacitors, an explicit state initialization switch, packet reset switches, and separate SKY130 transmission gates into each stored node. A positive top-plate step is applied to one packet capacitor and the equal negative step to the other; negative events reverse those signs. This direct differential charge transfer avoids the unqualified continuous CMFB loop. With100×100µm storage caps,1×1µm packet caps, and0.3V plate steps, one event produces +68.7µV TT, +75.4µV SS, and +55.0µV FF differential state (the ideal linear-cap estimate is60.0µV). The opposite sign produces matching negative deltas. A positive event at10µs remains through the5ms endpoint (4.99ms retention); a negative event at the same time also retains. Opposite `pn` events at10µs and4.5ms end at−0.1µV TT,−0.1µV SS, and0.0µV FF. Common mode at5ms is0.90004V TT,0.89991V SS, and0.90023V FF after using10/20µm NFET/PFET initialization devices with staggered release.

This is the strongest current state/injection candidate, not a qualified neuron module. The packet amplitude remains an ideal voltage command; only one packet pair / one site was simulated. No full-float event weights, fan-in, beta/threshold/reset, readout loading, mismatch/noise, extracted switch/interconnect, area scaling, energy, or5ms population service has passed. Positive/negative symmetry error to the ideal estimate varies by PVT (−8.3% FF, +14.2% SS, +14.5% TT), so analog weight mapping must use measured PVT/state transfer. Next lay out the paired state/packet capacitor and switch structure, extract it, and replay these tests through the extracted cell before mapping actual epoch58 events. Evidence/decks: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_differential_cap_packet/`; TT/SS/FF 5ms reports are `*_t1_10_t2_4500_*.json`.

### Shared-reset timing and reset-noise replay — 2026-10-02

Opening the inter-rail bridge while both VCM clamps remain on reduces the deterministic differential kick in the extracted TG/pair transient from 1.243µV to 0.132nV. The rails stay clamped for another 10µs before release. This is a sequencing result only: the deterministic simulator has no thermal noise or mismatch, and the independent-reset comparator also remains near zero in that model. It does not establish correlated reset noise. Reports and deck are `sky130_mos_pair_correlated_reset_ss0` and `sky130_mos_pair_correlated_reset_clamp_last_ss0`.

Replayed one full DSEC frame from the verified epoch58 full-float checkpoint with measured TT 5ms pair retention, ideal piecewise8 state encoding, and illustrative independent 24µV RMS differential reset noise. Three seeds with noise only at frame initialization gave 99.931–99.939% pixel agreement at range gain100, 99.918–99.919% at1000, and99.864% at10000. Reapplying the same noise after every subtractive spike reset gave99.927–99.935%,99.918–99.919%, and99.863–99.865%, respectively. Final-fusion spike mismatch remained below0.10%; labeled pixel accuracy remained near the float baseline49.82%. These results show the previous conclusion that 24µV reset noise necessarily destroys frame fidelity was tied to a less suitable state map/gain case. This replay remains a sensitivity test: piecewise encoding/update is ideal, the Gaussian is not a PDK transient-noise waveform, and the single frame does not qualify the circuit.

Next characterize the physical noise and state-dependent error of the extracted event injector against the original-float high-cancellation, maximum-fanin and near-threshold sequences. Build/extract a reset-and-state macro only after the injector and readout topology are credible; then run PVT/noise and replay measured errors. No full PLIF or chip acceptance is claimed.


### Extracted float-weight MOS injector sequence gate — 2026-10-02

Replayed the 322-event epoch58 high-cancellation final-fusion fan-in through the extracted SKY130 ten-device MOS state banks and 13-geometry signed injector at SS/0°C. With the existing ideal per-event bias commands, 4.4µs transfer pulses and 5µs slots, the shuffled full sequence ends at −49.77µV versus the full-float target +5.442µV: −55.22µV error and a final sign mismatch. The equivalent 40-event prefix ends −6.430µV from target; 2.5ns and10ns maximum timesteps agree within0.003µV, establishing numerical convergence for that prefix. A simple behavioral bidirectional state-feedback slope of0.004/V worsens the full shuffled error to−63.81µV. Measured-state-transfer correction on the first40 events worsens error to−102.56µV, while the ideal polarity correction leaves it near−6.59µV. These results do not support taking the current long-pulse MOS injector into a PLIF layout. They are bounded one-site analog screens, without physical weight DAC, mismatch/noise, threshold/reset/β or full network. Evidence is in `sky130_ms_mos_state_injector_boundary_high_cancellation_shuffle_layoutbank_dt10.json`, `_n40_dt2p5.json`, `_n40_dt10.json`, the state-feedback report and `_n40` correction reports.

Continue with the active differential charge bucket / switched-capacitor event path as the leading analog alternative. First characterize full-float weighted event order, near-threshold output, physical weight delivery, PVT, readout and retention together; reuse existing DRC/LVS-clean primitive cells where applicable. Do not scale the current MOS injector or claim a selected neuron macro yet.

### Active differential bucket CMFB sizing boundary — 2026-10-02

Repeated the 329-event epoch58 full-float near-threshold sequence at TT/27°C with extracted SKY130 TG capacitances. Without CMFB, the measured differential update is+37.78µV against+32.62µV target (error+4.97µV), while output common mode drifts from0.8983V to0.8661V. The stronger physical assist setting W/L=2/0.15µm holds common mode at0.8983V, but drives the final differential update to−182.66µV (error−218.40µV, wrong sign). It is not a usable servo setting. Thus the event path can produce the correct sign without the controller, but misses the selected float decision margin and has unbounded common-mode drift; the current two-independent-OTA bucket plus shared PMOS assist is rejected as a neuron macro. Evidence: `sky130_sc_integrator_diffpair_near_threshold_scale_full_shuffle_tt_physTGcap1.json` and `sky130_sc_integrator_diffpair_near_threshold_scale_full_shuffle_tt_physTGcap1_cmfbw2l0p15_rcm1e+08.json`.

Next architecture step is a true fully differential integrator with a common-mode loop inside its amplifier, so common-mode correction does not inject an uncontrolled differential current through separate output assist devices. Preserve the extracted MIM/TG primitives and original full-float event sequence. Before layout, screen that topology at TT/SS/FF for zero-event hold, signed event transfer, event-order sensitivity, and the near-threshold output decision. No transistor-level CMFB or physical macro is accepted yet.

### Active-bucket extracted-TG PVT and single-event diagnostics — 2026-10-02

For a +2.028mV ideal event, the output is+2.006mV TT and+2.065mV SS, but only+1.450mV FF; the FF output decays from+1.767mV at2.5ms to+1.450mV at5ms. At TT, the corresponding−3.105mV event measures−3.079mV. These results show state/event transfer and retention errors in addition to common-mode drift. Parameterised the existing OTA baseline-sample readout's held MIM capacitor and simulated a100×100µm cap for5ms at2ns maxstep, with one +/−10.5ns SKY130 MOS event and a no-event reference. ### Sampled-baseline regenerative readout: leakage-path correction and first buffer screen — 2026-10-02

The first integrated latch experiment used a 30kΩ/10kΩ reference divider from the floating baseline MIM node to VCM. This created a deliberate 40kΩ discharge path, so its measured baseline collapse was a schematic wiring error, not MIM/TG retention. The corresponding latch results are invalid. The second attempt removed that divider and directly connected signal/reference to the latch only during the final read aperture. This preserved the MIM baseline but directly loaded the sensor OTA; a closed-loop OTA follower was added between the sensor and the MIM/read switches.

With the follower at TT/27°C and a100µm MIM hold capacitor, the buffered signal is sampled at105µs and the latch is evaluated at4.999ms. The held baseline is0.975650V at105µs and0.975454V at5.000ms (−0.197mV total, including switch opening charge injection). Immediately before latch evaluation, the differential input is−1.518mV for the no-event case,−18.439mV for the positive MOS event and+15.275mV for the negative event. The latch output is respectively+1.793V,−1.793V and+1.792V: the positive event is separated from both zero and negative in this one-site TT screen. This is not accuracy or acceptance evidence; it omits model-derived thresholds, event sequences, mismatch/noise, extracted buffer/latch layout, and system scheduling.

At SS/0°C using the sensor's previously tuned0.55/0.658V biases and the same buffer biases, the follower/readout is not usable: the sampled baseline is1.314V, while the sensor output drifts from1.456V before sampling to0.985V at5ms; all three latch inputs land on the same side. An independent buffer-bias screen at0.45/0.55V did not correct this sensor drift. The shared-bias OTA follower is therefore not PVT-qualified. Next stabilize/retime the sensor's operating point, then repeat matched threshold and signed-event tests at TT/SS/FF. Do not lay out this composite macro yet. Evidence: `sky130_ms_diff_ota_autozero_th0_t5100u_s5n_mostt_temp27_bn0p65_bp0p75_hold100u_regen_buf_zero-pos06-neg06.json`, the SS fine-step counterpart, and the SS coarse buffer-bias screen.

### Full-float differential packet cell order/PVT gate — 2026-10-03

Ran the verified epoch58 full-float event weights through the DRC/LVS-qualified 100µm MIM state capacitor, 2µm packet-cap PCell, and extracted SKY130 TG PEX. For the selected 321-event final-fusion near-threshold site, a per-corner single-event calibration at0.3V and a common1.06072 injector gain gives final increment errors of+0.724nV TT, +3.884nV SS and+4.613nV FF. Arithmetic addition to the float-derived pre-event state and comparison with its ideal threshold predicts the same decision in this deterministic shuffle. The TT decision margin is only2.874nV after circuit update; these are not comparator simulations and exclude trim, mismatch, noise, weight storage and routing.

Event order fails decisively. At TT with the same calibrated gain and1.7µs packet pulse, the same 321 events give+4.067µV error in forward order and−5.189µV in reverse order; arithmetic threshold comparison predicts a false negative in reverse. A matched 4µs pulse/one-event calibration still gives+3.112µV/−5.624µV forward/reverse at unit injector gain. Compressing the sequence to a1ms event span, with calibration read at the5ms endpoint, gives−0.724µV on the shuffled order; it does not improve the default 4.89ms span. A zero-weight 322-slot control adds about0.0001µV, so repeated packet reset alone does not explain the weighted order effect. The current passive packet implementation is not a viable order-independent accumulator and is rejected for neuron/full-float macro layout.

The bounded 4× packet-cap PEX deck exceeded the300s ngspice limit for both a full sequence and a40-event prefix; its orphaned container was verified still running after4h and stopped. This is a simulator/runtime limit, not a physical-cap failure. Script updates add timestep- and calibration-phase-aware report lookup, optional packet-cap banks, pulse-width screening, and float-derived threshold-margin arithmetic. The last arithmetic comparison is diagnostic only. Evidence: `sky130_float_differential_cap_packet/{tt,ss,ff}_shuffle_near_threshold_321*`, `tt_{forward,reverse}_near_threshold_321*ig1.06072*`, the corresponding4µs-pulse reports, and the1ms-span report; runners are `scripts/test_sky130_differential_cap_packet.py` and `scripts/test_sky130_float_differential_cap_packet.py`.

Next build a true fully differential active charge integrator with common-mode feedback inside the amplifier and a low-impedance virtual summing node. Reuse the extracted MIM/TG cells and the same full-float high-cancellation and near-threshold traces. Gate its PVT results on order invariance, signed update accuracy,5ms retention, measured comparator margin, noise/mismatch and actor service before any integrated neuron layout. The earlier two-independent-OTA bucket, output-assist CMFB and shared-load-gate servo are rejected and are not reusable as evidence for this topology.

Continued the sampled-baseline latch diagnostic with the extracted MIM/TG sampler, a 2/4µm follower output stage, 100fF compensation, 10:1 symmetric latch-input attenuation, and FF/85°C. ### Active OTA-C virtual-ground debug gate — 2026-10-03

A fresh direct-current integrator bench was attempted to isolate virtual-ground feedback from the earlier switched-packet source. The 100nA, 10ns pulse deck did not produce any valid transient samples: ngspice aborted at the 50µs clamp release with the original ideal TG, then at 300µs event turn-on after that clamp was removed. Initializing the capacitor/output to 0.9V without an operating point instead aborted during startup at 5.50ns (`vbp_n#branch`). ngspice returned exit code 0 despite the aborted transient; the runner correctly rejects the run because the requested measurements are absent. A temporary 1GΩ DC feedback path did not resolve convergence. Therefore this branch is **not** circuit evidence and no Ceff/state result should be inferred from it.

The prior shared-output-load CMFB polarity check was also revisited. Normal polarity completed but ended at 0.956V TT, 1.152V SS and 1.012V FF after5ms. Reversing polarity rails every output near1.798–1.799V. Both fail the 0.9V common-mode target; neither is a candidate to layout. This narrows the problem to OTA bias/feedback topology and its transient convergence, rather than missing a Docker or PDK tool.

Reusable physical cells remain qualified individually (MIM state/packet capacitors and transmission gate with DRC/LVS and extracted-capacitance checks). An integrated OTA-C state cell is still unqualified. Continue by fixing the OTA DC operating point and common-mode loop in a compact single-cell transient first; require no-event startup/hold before adding event pulses, then signed pulses, PVT and actual float traces. Keep integrated macro layout gated until these simulations pass. Evidence: `scripts/test_sky130_diff_ota_current_integrator.py`, `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_diff_ota_current_integrator_tt_i100n_w10n.log`, and the shared-CMFB polarity qualification directories.

Follow-up debug on 2026-10-03: reversing the OTA input pins allowed a TT transient to complete, but both summing/output common nodes sat at only about1.4mV against the0.9V target; that run is an invalid configuration, not a virtual-ground pass. Restoring the documented OTA-C polarity yields a valid DC operating point near0.898V, but transient startup aborts at about4ns with timestep underflow. Increasing Miller compensation from0.5pF to5pF and then50pF did not resolve the abort. The earlier ideal-init-TG variant also failed at its control transition; using `.ic`/UIC without a clamp failed during startup. Across these configurations there is no valid integrated current-pulse/5ms result. The failure is now localized to OTA dynamic stability/initialization with the large feedback MIM load. Before the next pulse test, characterize the unloaded OTA small-signal/step response and find a stable bias/compensation point while preserving the documented negative-feedback pin order. Do not use the reversed-pin TT JSON as evidence.

Additional bounded TT diagnostic (2026-10-03): shrinking the feedback MIM to1µm×1µm lets the correctly polarized OTA transient finish, but at1nA×10ns the expected differential step is9.17mV while the measured step is0.293mV; the summing-node excursion reaches2.02mV and only22nV remains at5ms with1GΩ DC feedback. Raising the pulse to100nA saturates the tiny-cap case. A10µm×10µm cap with1TΩ DC feedback still aborts during startup. Thus merely reducing capacitance trades away charge gain and retention; this is not a viable size fix. The bench now writes explicit failed-run JSON and separately reports a 1mV virtual-ground criterion so simulator exit status cannot create a false pass. Continue from OTA bias/loop-stability design, not cap scaling.
