### 2026-10-03 single-ended OTA-C bias and full-float order follow-up

Corrected the full-float stimulus normalization: a single-ended storage branch receives the full `C·ΔV` charge; the earlier denominator of2 was appropriate only when driving equal/opposite differential branches. This exposed that a near-VCM summing node does not guarantee a correct stored-state update. A 20-instance shared-deck bias grid failed ngspice source stepping at `binitb#branch`; no data from that grid was retained, and the temporary runner was removed. Single-instance controls were used instead.

At `VBN=0.6406V`, `VBP=0.3528V`, `Rdc=1e15Ω`, `Cc=5pF`, the summing node remains within6.1µV of0.9V at measured checkpoints. The no-event output is0.8843–0.8850V and shifts+0.6532mV over the compressed event window. The same322-event epoch58 full-float trace (uniform1000× state scale) ends at+0.3727mV shuffled, +0.6042mV forward and+0.5482mV reverse relative to its pre-event state. Matched no-event checkpoint change is+0.6532mV, so baseline-subtracted event updates are−0.2805mV,−0.0490mV and−0.1050mV respectively versus the target+7.491µV. The order spread is≈231.5µV; this is a clear failure, despite good virtual-ground behavior.

Other diagnostics show one bias control can reduce baseline drift only while leaving the output offset large; changing the second control moves the offset but does not yet suppress drift. A `VBN=0.65V,VBP=0.37V` replay completes but its measured no-event drift is≈0.934mV and its baseline-subtracted shuffled update has the wrong sign. Wider100ns event pulses also worsen the shuffled residual. Conclusion: reject the present single-ended OTA-C implementation for layout. The corrected stimulus is retained in `scripts/test_sky130_ota_c_float_fanin.py`; its new no-event polarity option and startup output checkpoints support matched controls. Continue analog/full-float work with a topology or compensation method that preserves charge independently of state and event order. Existing standalone MIM/TG layout evidence remains reusable; integrated neuron layout, PVT, readout, mismatch/noise, retention, full-frame fidelity and population service remain open.

An exploratory non-VCM initialization at0.85V failed ngspice's initial transient solve under the current clamp sequence, so no state-range conclusion is drawn from it. The runner retains an initial-state parameter for a later initialization-ramp investigation; its default VCM case remains simulated successfully.

### 2026-10-03 OTA-C DC return and polarity-transfer screen

The single-ended OTA-C's DC return resistor controls both startup and state leakage. SKY130 DC sweeps at `VBN=0.6406V` show `Rdc=10GΩ` can place output near0.903V but leaves the summing node at0.864V; `100GΩ` brings the summing node to0.900004V and output to0.891771V at `VBP=0.35V`; `1TΩ` and `1PΩ` leave the output railed high at1.327V and1.755V in the DC sweep. These DC sweeps are not transient acceptance.

At `Rdc=100GΩ`, `Cc=5pF`, the single-instance no-event transient holds the summing node within2µV of VCM and output near0.925V, with+51.8µV baseline change over the checkpoint interval. The shuffled322-event high-cancellation sequence ends−124.9µV relative to its pre-event value; after subtracting the matched+51.8µV no-event change, the event contribution is−176.7µV versus a+7.491µV target. Thus the stronger DC return improves virtual-ground/startup behavior but does not pass float fidelity.

At the same bias, isolated positive and negative float events produce baseline-subtracted updates+47.4976mV and−47.8873mV against targets+43.7064mV and−43.6989mV, gains1.0867 and1.0958. The polarity error is measurable, but it does not explain the mixed-sequence order dependence by itself. A single signed-gain trim is insufficient evidence for acceptance. Non-VCM initial-state UIC testing settles near1.11V instead of retaining the requested0.85V, and event-gated OTA simulations still abort on a timestep underflow before measurements. Reject the present OTA-C implementation; no OTA-C layout has started. Continue with an analog autozero/common-mode or state-aware charge-transfer design and retain original float event traces as the gate. See `scripts/sweep_sky130_single_ota_bias.py`, `scripts/test_sky130_ota_c_float_fanin.py`, and reports `sky130_ota_c_float_fanin_single-ended_tt_shuffle_{none,both,positive,negative}_*`.

### 2026-10-03 analog R-2R buffer bias and transient follow-up

A six-point OTA bias screen across TT/SS/FF suggested a corner-specific analog bias: TT and FF use VBN/VBP=0.65/0.75V; SS uses0.55/0.65V. With a stronger output stage (`wn3=20µm`, `wp3=100µm`) and500fF compensation, the extracted TG32 plus extracted resistor ladder gives monotonic, in-rail DC output and maximum equal-step errors of0.101% TT,0.078% SS and0.054% FF. This is an isolated analog peripheral calibration; there is no weight QAT/quantization, realized bias generator or full-float event replay.

The transient test applies four analog codes with100ns break-before-make and compares against independent DC targets. The code0–2 outputs drift by several millivolts through each5µs hold and never stay within25µV of target. With500fF compensation only final code3 settles at TT/FF (128/67ns); no SS code settles. ### 2026-10-03 fixed-cap live two-range event crossing

The true 12-section ladder, with ideal resistors and PDK switches, passes the same 5ms q0.1 two-sign screen at SS/0°C (code2336), FF/85°C (code2026), and TT/27°C for both deterministic mirrored ±1σ latch mismatch orientations (codes2360 and1736). Maximum step was100ns. The sample-state kick remains≤0.064µV under the 1000× exploratory mapping. This is still a schematic trim ladder; the demonstrated code is per-corner/per-mismatch realization, with no actual calibration algorithm, resistor extraction/mismatch, switched noise or full-float trace replay. Reports are the four `sky130_regenerative_comparator_direct_mim_isolated_*_physicaltrim12_code*_bin5000u_step100n.json` artifacts. Next make a physical 12-bit R2R implementation only if extracted switch/resistor performance can be improved beyond the prior 2-bit ladder results; otherwise move the offset correction to a compact sampled-cap/autozero implementation.

### Physical 12-bit R-2R switch-linearity gate

Extended `scripts/analyze_sky130_r2r12_pdk_transfer.py` to sweep all4096 codes at a selected PVT point and load, and corrected DNL normalization to the actual 0.1V reference span. With 10kΩ R legs, unit SKY130 TGs (m=1), 0.85/0.95V rails and1MΩ output load, TT shows nonmonotonic carry transitions as large as−13.71mV. Scaling R legs to100kΩ still leaves a−1.71mV carry reversal. Raising the switch multiplicity to1000 makes the transfer monotonic in TT but leaves33.97 LSB max absolute error and requires an unrealistic thousandfold MOS width screen; none of these runs contains routed interconnect, resistor mismatch or extracted full-DAC layout. Reject the R-2R trim as a physical calibration path: its bit-switch resistance distorts carry boundaries, and the switch scale needed to recover monotonicity is too large. Move to local sampled-charge offset storage/autozero, reusing the extracted MIM/TG primitives, then rerun the same 5ms threshold cases before layout promotion. Machine reports use `sky130_r2r12_pdk_pdk_m{1,1000}_..._tt_t27c_load1e+06_transfer.json`.
### 2026-10-03 — sampled MIM offset-trim candidate and extracted unit check

Added a binary-weighted switched-charge trim experiment to `scripts/test_sky130_sc_buffered_readout_chop.py`. The coarse and fine banks use parallel minimum 2x2um SKY130 MIM cells; the fine bank uses a 0.5mV plate span. This is comparator calibration only and does not quantize model weights or states. The runner correctly instantiates physical capacitance by repeating unit devices because the SKY130 MIM `mf` parameter does not scale capacitance.

At TT/27°C, coarse code33/fine255 resolves both ±0.537257883µV (the measured float q0.1 margin) at the5ms endpoint under one deterministic +1σ/−1σ latch input mismatch pair. The mirrored −1σ/+1σ pair passes both signs at coarse222/fine0. Membrane read disturbance is zero at the report's 1nV resolution. These are four deterministic schematic-plate-drive latch screens; the bit controls are ideal, trim-array routing is absent, no noise/yield or PVT mismatch is included, and the tested codes sit at the fine-bank rails. They are feasibility results, not a layout or acceptance result. Reports are `sky130_sc_buffered_readout_chop_*_trimcap8c33_finecap8c255_bin5000u_step100n.json` and matching `trimcap8c222_finecap8c0` files.

Added `scripts/test_sky130_cap_offset_trim_unit.py` to combine already-routed unit MIM PCells with the extracted SKY130 TG PEX and simulate opposite differential plate steps at TT/27°C, SS/0°C and FF/85°C. The extracted differential step is44.79–45.15µV for a±50mV per-side plate change and0.224–0.227µV for±0.25mV, against analytic43.57µV and0.218µV. A single-ended switch experiment showed several microvolts of feedthrough at the fine step; the matched differential pair cancels it to the measured range. This unit test has no weighted capacitor array, mismatch/noise Monte Carlo, latch, or routed composite macro. It does not verify code carry, calibration headroom, hold noise, or full-float decisions.

Next, build a routed differential coarse/fine array with extracted switches and parasitics, characterize the whole code transfer and calibration margin across PVT, then rerun the5ms q0.1/full-float threshold traces with the extracted transfer. Keep the readout and neuron layout gated until those tests pass; full analog-neuron and population-service qualification remain open.

### 2026-10-04 — extracted weighted offset-trim array and latch-offset failure

Routed a 1020-cell SKY130 MIM coarse/fine differential array. Magic reports zero DRC and Netgen a unique LVS match; extracted PEX contains 280 parasitic capacitors. The macro is767.62×197.755µm. The 96 TG selector devices and decoder are still schematic-connected and not part of this GDS.

Integrated the array PEX into the buffered chopper/readout transient with PEX sampling MIMs and 96 PEX selector TGs. At a2mV coarse plate span and midpoint code, both chopper input signs remain correct and the recorded state disturbance is zero at report resolution. The deterministic ±1σ latch mismatch pair nevertheless rejects the negative q0.1 case. With10mV coarse span, trim sweeps the pre-evaluation latch gate differential beyond±4.7mV without changing the wrong latch decision; without the mismatch variants, the negative case passes. A separate SKY130 sensor-pair screen reports a2.13mV input-referred crossing. This is a failed direct-regenerative-latch robustness screen, not a readout pass. Use a preamplified and/or autozeroed comparator, verify offset at the actual latch aperture, then route selector/decoder and rerun five-ms, PVT and full-float decision checks.

#### Follow-up — common TT code found; PVT readout failure remains

A single physical-array code now passes both ±0.537257883µV classifications at5ms TT/27°C under the same deterministic ±1σ latch mismatch pair: coarse37/fine80, with20mV coarse and0.1mV fine plate spans. Both runs include extracted weighted-array capacitance PEX and96 PEX TG instances. The array's state disturbance is zero at report resolution. Chopper input sign is correct at TT, but input-equivalent readout errors are−0.321µV/−0.265µV; this is a sign-only comparator result, not model-value accuracy or full-float acceptance.

The fixed code fails SS/0°C and FF/85°C: the positive test input yields negative chopper output and the latch decision is wrong. The SS polarity error persists at10ns max step, at1.05V common mode, and with the high-resistor variant. Therefore the TT pass is not PVT robust. The prior physical trim array remains DRC/LVS/PEX qualified, but its96 TG/decoder selector layout is open. Resolve or replace the buffered chopper front end before completing the switch-array layout; then rerun both q0.1 signs across PVT, noise/mismatch, and the original full-float trace.
### 2026-10-04 direct MIM trim-pair PEX transfer across corners

Continued the routed differential SKY130 MIM trim-pair characterization with the extracted pair, extracted 30µm-square state/sample MIM banks, and extracted PDK transmission gates. At a small ±0.1mV plate excursion, the measured differential state correction is 0.09426–0.09470µV across SS/TT/FF at 27°C, versus 0.08714µV ideal. At the full rail-safe ±0.5V plate excursion (1.4V/0.4V plates around 0.9V VCM), the correction is 455.74–456.01µV across SS/TT/FF, versus 435.71µV ideal. The measured transfer varies by <0.06% across these three device corners, and the extraction includes pair coupling capacitances.

This qualifies the two-cell trim primitive's bounded PEX transfer only. Its maximum correction is below the several-millivolt deterministic latch-offset corrections previously needed by the direct regenerative comparator, so this two-cell trim pair alone cannot calibrate that latch while driving the present large sample/state capacitance. No decoder, full weighted cap bank, latch integration, transient noise, mismatch yield, temperature sweep, or float-network replay is included. Next combine the calibrated PEX transfer with a larger extracted charge-trim bank or a lower-capacitance comparator input, then measure usable correction range and input disturbance before promoting the direct readout path. The buffered-chopper path remains rejected at SS/FF polarity.

#### Direct comparator integration diagnostic — trim bank disturbs state

Integrated the routed 1,020-cell weighted MIM trim-array PEX into `scripts/test_sky130_regenerative_comparator_direct_mim_isolated.py`, with full-float-derived ±0.537257883µV source cases (mapped 1,000× for simulator observability), 90mV coarse and0.4mV fine plate spans, and ideal plate-selection waveforms. Python syntax and the bounded TT transient completed. In the positive 30µs diagnostic case, the expected latch polarity was obtained, but the stored differential state changed from537.288µV before read to1,899.243µV after trim/read:1,361.955µV disturbance. The direct connection does not isolate the state capacitor from array loading and plate switching; this topology is rejected, and its decision result is not a readout pass.

Next place the trim bank on a buffered/isolated comparator sample node and prove the membrane remains disconnected during trim switching. Then measure trim range/resolution, state disturbance, and latch polarity at TT/SS/FF at the5ms endpoint, followed by noise/mismatch and original-float replay. The present result is TT-only, one sign, short-bin, deterministic, with ideal selector controls and no switch-selector/decoder GDS.

#### Buffered trim calibration at SS and FF, 5 ms endpoint

Used the existing isolated buffered-readout bench and the routed weighted trim-array PEX, with ideal plate-selection waveforms, to calibrate the deterministic SS/0°C and FF/85°C corners separately. A local coarse/fine search found SS code125/231 and FF code125/220 (20mV coarse and0.1mV fine spans). At each corner the same code classifies both mapped±0.537257883µV cases correctly at5ms; both runs per corner preserve membrane state to the report resolution. The SS and FF codes differ, so a fixed factory code does not transfer across these corners; a per-circuit calibration mechanism would be needed.

This closes only a deterministic, binary latch classification screen for one tiny mapped input pair. The chopper's reported analog value remains wrong-sign for the positive input at both corners and its input-equivalent error is several microvolts, so it cannot yet support accurate analog membrane readout. Selector TGs/decoder are schematic/ideal controls and lack GDS; there is no latch mismatch/noise Monte Carlo, yield, process-temperature grid, full-float threshold replay, or system service evidence. Next establish a calibrated analog-value or threshold readout with margin, make the calibration controls physical, then run mismatch/noise and full-float traces before module promotion.

Extended temperature calibration at SS/27°C: code126/55 (same20mV coarse and0.1mV fine spans) also passes both mapped input signs at the5ms endpoint, with state disturbance0 at report resolution. Chopped analog outputs are+0.662µV and−9.135µV against symmetric expected values±5.426µV; the polarity is now correct, but a several-microvolt offset and gain error remain. Thus threshold sign can be calibrated at another temperature point, while analog-value fidelity has not been demonstrated. This is still a single deterministic mismatch-free comparator instance with ideal plate-selection controls and no full-float replay.

An exactly zero-differential SS/27°C run at code126/55 does not resolve: ngspice transient analysis underflows at19.8431µs on the latch clock branch as the regenerative latch reaches metastability. This is a real calibration boundary for this test bench; do not use a zero-input latch result as a stable code-search decision. A physical calibration sequence must use bounded positive/negative dither or hysteresis and quantify the resulting deadband against original-float threshold margins.

#### SS/27°C trim resolution and front-end gain probe

At SS/27°C with coarse126/fine53, the buffered PEX bench classifies both±0.25µV and both±0.537257883µV mapped inputs correctly in the30µs screen; both±0.537257883µV also pass at5ms. The same code's5ms chopped outputs are+0.6605/−9.1333µV versus expected±5.4263µV, so the latch sign gate passes while value readout retains strong offset/asymmetry. The exactly-zero run aborts at latch metastability as recorded above. The±0.25 tests are short-bin only, have no noise/mismatch, and do not define the continuous margin distribution.

Added a `--gain` resistor-ratio override to the bench and tested9.0,10.1 and11.2 at SS/27°C on the positive mapped q0.1 point. The raw chopped result changes only modestly (about0.62–0.66µV) while expected scales4.84–6.02µV; input-referred error stays near−0.47µV. This rejects simple closed-loop resistor-ratio scaling as a repair for this operating point. Keep the gain parameter for characterization, but do not promote it as a calibration result. Next diagnose the buffered chopper's dominant input-referred offset/gain nonlinearity and implement a physical offset-calibration/control path; PEX plate selection, switched noise, mismatch and full-float replay remain open.

2026-10-04 follow-up — bounded isolated MIM population sweep: added `--device-limit` support to `scripts/diagnose_sky130_trim_array_state_drift.py` and ran 1, 16, 256, 512, 640, 704, 736, 768, 900, and 1020 standard SKY130 MIM instances with every plate and both state rails ideally clamped at 0.9 V. One/16 cells produce only floating-point-noise clamp current; 256/512 produce about 18–20 pA; the ordered prefix then climbs (640:≈89 pA on `sa`, 704:≈124 pA, 736:≈140 pA, 768+:≈157 pA). This narrows the symptom to a cumulative/model-population effect in the middle portion of the instance list, not an isolated selector or a single bad cell. Removing `dev/gauss` and tightening `gmin` do not remove the full-array symptom. A diagnostic with the PDK via series resistor changed to exactly 0 Ω also produced near-zero isolated current, but the integrated transient became stiff and timed out at 180 s around 0.09 ms; reject it as a usable fix. Continue by correlating the first current jump against per-bank/device parameters and running controlled device-model experiments; preserve unmodified PDK as signoff basis. 2026-10-04 model correlation update: analyzed the ordered `reference.spice` prefix sweep by state-bank ownership. 255 coarse-P cells alone give≈18pA on `sa`; adding 255 coarse-N cells leaves about≈18pA per rail; fine-bank prefixes increase the observed current (640 devices:≈89pA on `sa`; 704:≈124pA; 736:≈140pA; full:≈157pA per rail). Each 255-cell bank isolated independently gives≈18pA, so no individual bank/device subset reproduces the full symptom. Tying all plate terminals together does not remove it; tying all state terminals together reduces but does not eliminate it (≈122pA). This is now clearly a many-instance/model-network interaction in ngspice/PDK behavior; the exact cause remains unproven. Diagnostic zero-R bypass remains rejected due stiffness. Continue with paired-bank/subcircuit scaling and an independent simulator/reference check; no arbitrary PDK edits to production netlists.

These are area and noise feasibility bounds, not measured switched error rates or an accepted analog precision target. The deterministic six-case PVT readout still stands as a module demonstrator. Stop scaling this sample/trim readout for chip assembly and prioritize a compact analog state/sensor organization, then require switched-noise, mismatch, full-float replay and 5ms schedule measurements. Reproducible source-hashed calculation: `scripts/analyze_sky130_readout_sample_noise_service.py` and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_readout_sample_noise_service_gate.json`.

2026-10-04 fixed-cap live-range branch probe: added optional zero-volt series current probes to the existing PDK schematic two-range comparator/PFET/TG bench and completed one positive crossing at each TT/27C, SS/0C and FF/85C, with externally fitted boundary trim and ideal pulse gate. Integrated fast/slow charges are5.829/2.679fC at TT (1.0V gate,1.5us),7.869/3.366fC at SS (0.9V gate,2us), and6.306/64.850fC at FF (1.0V gate,1.5us). The matched no-event state updates are8.840/11.536/84.677mV. A100mV increase of FF gate level to1.1V reduces its event update to21.436mV and changes the detected selector edge to71ns before nominal state crossing, at1.641mV below the boundary. These are PDK current and transient observations, not full-float weight charge accuracy: one positive event, different externally chosen gate levels, no physical bias/calibration, signed negative event, long hold, noise/mismatch, PEX or layout. The large FF gate sensitivity and changed comparator timing make joint charge/range calibration the next physical problem. Source-hashed case summary: `scripts/summarize_sky130_piecewise_branch_charge.py` and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_piecewise_branch_charge_summary.json`.

2026-10-04 full-float sensor-noise sensitivity: the verified epoch58 original float model was replayed on two complete DSEC validation frames (all neurons, three seeds per frame) under an *ideal* six-range piecewise state encoder, the measured SS/0C differential MOS/TG 5ms retention profile and independent24.80086uV RMS threshold-noise proxy derived from the existing SS/0C 100-device direct-sensor small-signal spectrum over10Hz–1MHz. Record0 means are86.35% pixel agreement,2.608% final-fusion spike mismatch and0.20402 present-class IoU versus0.20573 float; record1 gives85.25%,2.767% and0.20080 versus0.19892 float. Every278,784,000 counted state accesses per frame lies inside the measured retention profile. This demonstrates task-level tolerance to this *ideal noise proxy* on two frames; it does not validate the physical sensor or the six-range event update. The replay lacks switched noise/correlation, systematic offset/mismatch, physical encoding and signed charge, actual 5ms service, and validation-set statistics. Machine summary with source hashes: `scripts/summarize_sky130_companded_sensor_noise_replay.py` and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_companded_sensor_noise_replay_summary.json`.

2026-10-04 MIM state-size consistency check: the live two-range physical bench stores one state on a20x20um MIM, while the preceding full-frame retention replay used a *different* MOS gate-bank state. The routed2x2um MIM unit's9.52fF capacitance density implies≈0.952pF for20x20um and a one-cap300K kT/C reset-noise proxy65.96uV; one bare20x20um plate for each30,976,000 logical states is12,390mm2 before enclosure/switches. On the original-float model with ideal six-range encoding, independent reset-noise and *zero retention loss*, two-frame three-seed mean pixel agreement is84.35%/83.11%, final-fusion spike mismatch3.07%/3.27%, and present-class IoU0.20361/0.20085 versus float0.20573/0.19892. For one minimum2x2um MIM per state, the corresponding kT/C proxy is659.60uV, bare plate area123.90mm2, pixel agreement80.15%/78.67%, spike mismatch4.48%/4.75%, and IoU0.20129/0.19769. These ideal sensitivity replays make small-state physical experiments worthwhile; neither size is accepted because the required state-range gain, retention, signed event update, readout, and full accuracy are absent. 2026-10-04 minimum physical MIM retention gate: reused the DRC0/LVS-unique routed differential2x2um MIM pair as two state cells and the routed TG PEX for a5ms SS/0C hold at0.6/1.2V. The raw extracted MIM PEX has an unconnected internal `VSUBS` parasitic node; runs with it floating show hundreds of millivolts of *ideal-switch* drift and cannot be used as leakage measurements. Diagnostic grounding of `VSUBS` plus reduced-order nominal MIM C makes ideal-switch control drift0mV, while the actual routed TG states drift+165.81/−149.24mV from2us to5ms. With the standard unmodified PDK MIM model and grounded substrate, TG drift+166.45/−152.87mV and ideal controls drift−1.96/−3.96mV, supporting a switch-dominated failure. Two series routed TGs with grounded substrate/reduced MIM drift+179.22/−155.98mV, so this particular stacked switch does not repair hold. The routed two-MIM *pair* bbox23.62x3.01um would occupy a1,101mm2 replication floor if its two cells each stored one of30.976m states, even before switches, routing, sensor or state-update circuitry. The earlier larger MOS gate-bank macro instead uses one differential pair *per* state; its actual239.85x101.99um bbox replicates to≈757,744mm2, consistent with the project specification's≈0.76m2 estimate. A linearized1mV hold-error limit for the small MIM would demand an illustrative≈30us perfect refresh interval for the worst state, about1.03e12 refresh operations/s across the inventory; no refresh circuit or schedule exists. Reject this minimum MIM+TG as an unrefreshed5ms membrane. Grounded-substrate and reduced-model deck modifications are diagnostic, not PDK signoff. See `scripts/screen_sky130_min_mim_tg_hold.py`, `scripts/summarize_sky130_min_mim_hold_boundary.py`, and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_min_mim_hold_boundary_summary.json`.

2026-10-04 off-switch isolation follow-up: physically meaningful ideal programming of the routed2x2um MIM pair to0.6/1.2V (with grounded PEX substrate) removed an invalid `.ic`/`uic` startup that had put a state above the1.8V rail. In a corrected SS/0C reduced-MIM deck, the bare state holds exactly; the routed off TG moves+156.03/−155.66mV over5ms. An off schematic minimum-width NFET alone moves−220.53/−437.91mV, while an off minimum-width PFET alone moves+436.89/+219.76mV. Increasing either single-device channel length from0.5 to8um changes those large drifts by less than0.05mV (NFET) or0.03mV (PFET). A schematic longer-channel TG still moves+176.06/−175.89mV. Repeating with the unmodified MIM model and grounded substrate gives the same directional and order-of-magnitude behavior; bare-state solver drift is−1.87/−3.74mV, so the reduced-model control is the cleaner comparison. This rules out the tested simple long-channel isolation fix and identifies the off device as the dominant hold disturbance, without assigning internal junction versus channel current. Source-hashed reports: `scripts/screen_sky130_min_mim_offswitch_leakage.py` and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_min_mim_hold_boundary_summary.json`.

2026-10-04 open-process check: the [ICsprout55 PDK repository](https://github.com/openecos-projects/icsprout55-pdk) still labels its55nm release preview and lists SPICE models, RAMs and RC extraction as to-do items despite broader introductory language about device models. The [OpenRPDK28 repository](https://github.com/RIOSLaboratory/OpenRPDK28/blob/main/README.md) says it is under construction. [IHP's open design enablement](https://www.ihp-microelectronics.com/services/research-and-prototyping-service/open-source-design-enablement) provides a real analog SG13G2 flow but at130nm, and its [published MIM density](https://www.ihp-microelectronics.de/fileadmin/images/mediathek/annual_reports/annualreport_ihp_2024.pdf) is about1.5fF/um2, below the locally measured SKY130 unit density2.38fF/um2. This is a current public-collateral screen, not a foundry-capability claim. There is no source-backed reason here to migrate the present physical analog bench to a denser *ready* open PDK. Keep SKY130 as the circuit evidence platform while the analog state architecture remains unresolved.

### 2026-10-05 signed fixed-cap live-range crossing

Extended `scripts/test_sky130_piecewise_fixedcap_crossing.py` to use a physical NFET sink branch and reverse comparator polarity for negative events on the same fixed 20x20um PDK MIM topology. Four source-hashed, completed one-event schematic cases are consolidated by `scripts/summarize_sky130_piecewise_signed_crossing.py` in `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_piecewise_signed_crossing_summary.json`. At ideal externally fitted gates and comparator references, positive TT/27C gives+8.840mV at gate1.0V; negative TT/27C gives-9.364mV at gate0.4V; negative SS/0C gives-8.568mV at gate0.45V; negative FF/85C gives-9.785mV at gate0.305V. Each dynamic result lies between its same-pulse static fast/slow controls. The selector state at the nominal boundary is within about13uV for these individually tuned cases. The TT negative/positive magnitude ratio is1.059, but these are different tuned gates and initial states, so it is not a matched-weight symmetry result.

The 1.5us/event pulse, manual corner-dependent gate settings, and fitted comparator reference remain unimplemented controls. There is no physically delivered float weight, positive/negative pair sequence on one stored state, noise/mismatch, PEX, 5ms retention, or PLIF beta/threshold/reset/adaptation. A single max-fanin site with370 serial events would spend at least555us on these pulses, before gate setup, readout, and the other actors in the 5ms DAG; this is a local lower bound, not a graph service pass. Continue with matched signed event sequences and a physical programmable bias/feedback path, while retaining the independent storage and readout gates. Do not promote this schematic crossing cell to layout acceptance.

A subsequent same-capacitor direct-injection screen (`scripts/test_sky130_piecewise_signed_sequence.py`) applies two 1.5us physical PFET/NFET pulses in `pp`, `pn`, `np`, and `nn` order, with separate single-event and no-event cells. It intentionally excludes the range selector to isolate injector polarity and state dependence. TT/27C gate commands1.0V positive and0.3678V negative yield+9.0549/−9.1195mV individual updates; `pn`/`np` leave−0.0791/−0.0624mV after no-event drift subtraction, a16.7uV order difference. At the same gates, SS/0C gives+0.9404/−1.8144mV and `pn`−0.8743mV; FF/85C gives+127.0311/−76.5968mV and `pn`+49.0046mV. The equal-weight TT calibration is therefore strongly corner-specific. In FF the `pn` versus `np` order difference is−1.6569mV, also material for small float margins. The same-corner two-event nonadditivity is small at TT (−14.5/+2.2uV for `pn`/`np`) relative to the cross-corner polarity error. Source-hashed machine summary: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_piecewise_signed_sequence_corner_summary.json`. This is a deterministic schematic diagnostic with ideal gate sources, no live selector, learned-weight map, 5ms storage, PEX, or network semantics; it narrows the next driver task to physical adaptive/corner-calibrated charge delivery.

Fitting ideal gates separately at the same0.9V initial state narrows the corner problem but does not qualify the driver: SS/0C gate0.9V positive/0.4271V negative gives+9.6202/−9.6377mV, `pn`−35.2uV and `np`−14.7uV; FF/85C gate1.16V/0.2732V gives+10.3057/−10.3199mV, `pn`−30.6uV and `np`−11.5uV. The fitted `pn`/`np` order spread remains about17–21uV across TT/SS/FF, above the exploratory submicrovolt float decision margin. These gate pairs are manual ideal voltage sources, each calibrated to this single state and pulse width. Physical bias generation, gain tracking over state/weight range, mismatch, and long sequences are still open.

The corrected minimum-MIM hold summary now includes a conditional fixed-current area extrapolation. The measured worst grounded-PEX SS drift and approximately10.97fF state capacitance imply a time-averaged3.64e-13A off-switch current over5ms. Holding that current fixed while increasing only MIM capacitance requires about1.82pF and23,674mm2 bare plate area across30.976m states for1mV drift; 18.2pF/236,735mm2 for100uV; or303pF/3.95m2 for6uV. These are *conditional design-pressure estimates*, not physical lower bounds: switch current depends on voltage, time, and geometry, and the screen covers one corner and two held voltages. They exclude switches, access, sensing, noise, and routing. They explain why simply enlarging the present leaky MIM+TG cell is unlikely to meet the full-float density target; a different isolation/storage mechanism is still needed.

Near-VCM follow-up changes the interpretation of that wide-range current extrapolation. With the same routed minimum MIM/TG and grounded-substrate reduced model, ±1mV commanded state offsets from0.9V drift toward VCM by+0.5216/−0.5175mV over5ms; ±10mV states drift+5.1983/−5.1939mV. The bare MIM controls drift0mV. Thus about52% of the **stored offset** decays over5ms at both small amplitudes. The wide-range fixed-current area estimates above must not be applied to this compressed regime; the measured leakage current itself falls as the state approaches VCM. A predictable offset decay might be mapped into an analog state transformation, but the model applies learned beta once at each bin boundary and accumulates events throughout the bin, so such a mapping would need a physical event-time correction and actual full-float replay. It is not established here. The unrefreshed TG state still fails retention of microvolt-scale information. Source hashes and fractions are in `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_min_mim_hold_boundary_summary.json`.

An explicit event-time diagnostic then injected the same ideal11pA×1us charge onto that routed off-TG cell at0.5ms or4.5ms, with a matched no-event branch. The original1us maximum transient step falsely made the late pulse appear larger; it is superseded. At both100ns and50ns maximum steps the immediate post-pulse increments agree within0.1% (0.8075mV early,0.8068mV late). At5ms the increments are0.4174mV early and0.7499mV late, a0.557 ratio. This establishes a large event-arrival-time dependence for this accessed state cell, contrary to the full-float bin accumulation contract. The pulse source is ideal and the reduced PEX is diagnostic; no physical event driver or system replay passes. The source-hashed50ns report is included in the same minimum-MIM hold boundary summary.

The source-hashed original epoch58 float export contains1,856 per-channel learned beta values: min0.323, q10 0.928, median0.938, q90 0.945, max0.975; only6 are below0.6 and1,804 are at least0.9. These are unweighted channel counts, not resident-state counts. The near-VCM MIM/TG cell retains about0.48 of a small stored offset over5ms, so its continuous physical leak cannot simply stand in for the learned beta on most channels. More critically, it attenuates early and late contributions unequally before the one learned-beta operation at the bin boundary. A compensated analog architecture would need to preserve that order or demonstrate task-level accuracy after a deliberate changed-model retraining; neither exists.

Finally, two routed off TGs in series were screened at the same near-VCM offsets after ideal state programming. At±1mV the stacked cell drifts+0.5219/−0.5178mV versus+0.5216/−0.5175mV for one TG; at±10mV it drifts+5.2009/−5.1964mV versus+5.1983/−5.1939mV. The added series TG provides no observable retention benefit in this diagnostic PEX model, consistent with the previous wide-range stacked screen. It also adds switch area and programming complexity, so do not carry this simple stack into a state macro.
## Isolated body-tied SKY130 switch and well-leakage boundary — 2026-10-05

The low-voltage isolated-well TG probe has a 6.25×16.345µm GDS bounding box, Magic DRC0, unique Netgen LVS, and 25 extracted coupling capacitors. Both NFET p-well and PFET n-well have separate body ports; a DNW contact has a separate bias port. The body-to-state metal connections and external DNW bias are made only in the hold testbench, not in the GDS. Magic PEX includes 3.37fF p-well-to-DNW coupling but no junction leakage. The reduced 2×2µm MIM pair uses nominal capacitances plus extracted parasitics and a grounded substrate; the standard PDK MIM model's unexplained bare-control drift remains unresolved.

At TT/27°C, ±1mV state offsets from0.9V drift by only −0.2/−0.3µV over5ms in the physical TG PEX without the well diode. Adding the PDK `sky130_fd_pr__model__parasitic__diode_pw2dn` with a provisional 35.75µm² area/24µm perimeter based on the DNW rectangle changes those drifts to +5.947/+5.934mV, or −12.8µV differential drift. Increasing the high-side diode area and perimeter by1% gives +41.7µV differential drift; this is an illustrative sensitivity, not a measured mismatch distribution. At ±300mV the matched diode-proxy case drifts +7.9125/+3.9650mV, or −3.9475mV differential. Biasing DNW to0.902V for the ±1mV states reduces common-mode drift to8.45µV but leaves −16.7µV differential drift. The SS/0°C and FF/85°C 1.8V-DNW matched diode cases have +5.891/+5.878mV and +1.091/+1.088mV low/high drift, respectively. The reversed apparent corner trend is what this PDK/model/bench produced; no temperature law or signoff is inferred.

The standalone TT diode current probe reports about34.4fA for that provisional geometry at0.9V reverse bias. Its diode area encoding is directly exercised in ngspice, but ngspice warns that several PDK diode-card parameters are unrecognized; the physical junction geometry, lateral leakage, guard-ring behavior, latchup and foundry DRC are also not extracted or qualified. These results reject the present compact body-tied TG as a 5ms full-float membrane store; the small PEX-only drift must not be used as retention evidence. Layout and bench: `scripts/build_sky130_body_tied_transmission_gate.py`, `scripts/screen_sky130_body_tied_tg_pex_hold.py`, `artifacts/analog_asic/layout/sky130_body_tied_transmission_gate/qualification_probe.json`, and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_body_tied_tg_pex_hold_tt_temp27_offset{1,300}mv.json`. Continue analog storage research with a topology that removes the continuously connected well junction, then test real state write/read, switched noise, mismatch, signed event ordering, PLIF update and 30.976M-state service before macro replication.

Increasing the fixed-body schematic TG channel length from0.5 to8µm scarcely changes SS/0°C +1mV-side drift over5ms: +0.5875→+0.5869mV. A source-hashed area summary scales the actual isolated TG probe bounding box (102.156µm²) without sharing: one TG for each of two differential rails across30,976,000 states is6,328.8mm², plus247.8mm² for two bare2×2µm MIM plates per state. This is a conditional unshared footprint, neither an optimized floorplan nor a physical lower bound. Under the additional fixed-leakage/inverse-capacitance assumption, reducing the illustrative1% diode-geometry case's41.7µV error to3µV would require13.9× the current ~10.97fF per-rail capacitance;3µV is an exploratory probe, not an acceptance threshold. Evidence: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_body_tied_tg_storage_boundary.json`.

The more complete junction proxy adds the opposite-polarity PFET n-well/substrate diode and subtracts the DNW bias strip from the NFET p-well area. The drawn-region estimates are27.625µm²/21.5µm for the p-well/DNW diode and8.8409µm²/12.6µm for the PFET n-well/substrate diode. At TT/27°C with ±1mV states, both diode proxies together give +3.1217/+3.1077mV low/high drift (−14.0µV differential); an illustrative1% high-side p-well junction enlargement gives+29.8µV differential. At ±300mV the matched case gives+5.158/+1.067mV, or−4.091mV differential. The outer-DNW geometry assumption gives−16.9µV and−4.957mV differential, respectively. These results supersede the single-diode estimate as the current *screening* proxy; neither uses extracted diode geometry or measured mismatch. The latest `sky130_body_tied_tg_storage_boundary.json` hashes each result. The older one-diode area multiplier above is retained only as a labeled conditional example, not a selected design rule.

Biasing DNW at1.2V balances the two diode currents near0.9V: the ±1mV pair has only+0.02605mV common-mode drift at TT/27°C, but still−14.3µV differential drift over5ms. At ±300mV with the same bias, common-mode drift is+0.0234mV while differential drift is−4.1008mV. The cancellation is therefore narrow and does not qualify the full state range; a1% geometry perturbation happens to cancel the tiny-offset differential in this one simulation, demonstrating trim sensitivity rather than measured tolerance. The [official SKY130 device notes](https://github.com/google/skywater-pdk/blob/main/docs/rules/device-details.rst) list SONOS cells with100,000 guaranteed program/erase cycles. At one membrane rewrite per5ms that endurance corresponds to500s, so SONOS is not a credible continuously updated resident membrane even before analog precision and public model coverage are evaluated. It may be relevant to static weights as a separate question.

**Shared-bus correction:** all low-drift body-tied hold numbers immediately above used an off-switch source parked at each rail's starting voltage. That hides the off-channel response to a changing state and does not represent a shared 0.9V source bus. With the source fixed at VCM and DNW1.2V, the TT/27°C ±1mV pair and both well-diode proxies drift+0.3920/−0.3395mV over5ms: differential span falls from1.9998mV to1.2683mV (36.6% loss). The ±300mV pair drifts+175.028/−178.229mV: span falls from599.1998mV to245.9428mV (58.9% loss). Common-mode drift is small because the two junction currents are balanced, but this does not preserve membrane information. Both cases retain the same ideal initialization and reduced MIM model as the parked-source control. These shared-bus runs supersede the parked-source values for architectural selection.

Equal ideal11pA×1µs charge commands on the shared-bus pair at0.5ms and4.5ms give nearly equal immediate differential increments (~0.474mV), but at5ms the early and late increments relative to a matched no-event branch are0.3154/0.4533mV (ratio0.696) with50ns maximum step. Repeating at25ns gives0.3152/0.4549mV (ratio0.693); the event-time dependence persists under step refinement. This is another failure against the original float model's within-bin accumulation contract. The event source and programming are ideal; physical write, sensor, transistor mismatch and task-level replay are absent. The first attempted measurement deck failed because ngspice did not accept a two-node voltage expression in `meas`; the completed decks measure both rails separately. Canonical inputs/results are in `scripts/screen_sky130_body_tied_tg_event_time.py` and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_body_tied_tg_storage_boundary.json`, which hashes both step sizes and shared-bus hold reports.

A passive floating guard between two extracted body-tied TGs was then screened with the same ideal initialization and event current. A10fF guard per rail improves the early/late final-increment ratio to0.773 (0.3593/0.4647mV); a100fF guard yields0.718 (0.3275/0.4559mV), so simple guard-cap enlargement is not monotonic or sufficient. Neither case co-writes the guard with each event, and neither has physical guard capacitor layout. Four copies of the current TG probe per differential state, without sharing, multiply to12,657.6mm² across30.976M states before guard/state capacitors or routing. This is a conditional footprint, not a lower bound. Do not build a population macro from passive guard stacking; an active guard would need a real per-event tracking/restore mechanism, its own precision and service proof. The latest source-hashed boundary JSON includes both runs.

A 2026-10-05 public-PDK recheck finds no ready small-node analog migration: [ICsprout55 still lists SPICE models and RC extraction as pending](https://github.com/openecos-projects/icsprout55-pdk), while [OpenRPDK28 labels itself under construction](https://github.com/RIOSLaboratory/OpenRPDK28/blob/main/README.md). [IHP SG13G2](https://github.com/IHP-GmbH/IHP-Open-PDK) remains an available 130nm analog PDK, not an automatic density solution for this state store. Keep process changes contingent on a usable device/capacitor/leakage/extraction stack and a measured area/noise/service advantage.

## Fixed-bus small-MIM correction screen — 2026-10-05

The conventional routed 2×2µm MIM pair plus extracted TG was rerun with both off-switch sources fixed to one 0.9V bus, after ideal independent state programming. At symmetric ±1mV and ±300mV initial offsets, 5ms differential retention is 0.4744–0.4804 across TT/27°C, SS/0°C and FF/85°C. Inverting this deterministic differential decay would amplify any state-read error by 2.081–2.108 before writeback error. At FF/85°C near VCM, the two rails also acquire about +42.6/+43.6mV drift, leaving a +43.1mV common-mode shift that a real differential sensor must tolerate. The bare-MIM controls hold their programmed states. The source-hashed per-corner values and exact inverse gains are in `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_fixed_bus_hold_correction_boundary.json`.

This screen shows only that a two-point deterministic inverse is algebraically conditioned near 2.1; it does not validate a restore circuit. There is no physical read/write sequence, noise or mismatch, state-dependent transfer interpolation, event-bucket integration, full-float replay or 30.976M-state service. The existing 30µs large sampled readout already requires 185,856 zero-overhead channels and 4,751mm² in sample-bank bounding boxes, before writeback. Do not promote the minimum-MIM/TG state to resident layout on the basis of this correction. The next analog gate is a much smaller differential sensor and real boundary read/restore schedule, or an isolation topology with adequate measured 5ms retention.

## Passive sensor-gate storage clue and power-up failure — 2026-10-05

Attaching a schematic 10×1µm NMOS input gate to each rail of the extracted minimum MIM/TG, with a fixed shared 0.9V off-switch bus, raises the ±1mV 5ms differential retention from 0.474–0.480 without a sensor to 0.894–0.896 when the sensor tail and supply are both held at 0V. TT/27°C, SS/0°C and FF/85°C all complete. The continuously biased sensor gives 0.894–0.908 retention and a responsive DC output, but draws 27.83µA from 1.8V at TT. Direct always-on replication over 30,976,000 states would be about 1,552W of sensor supply power alone, a conditional multiplication that disqualifies that architecture. Two 10×1µm NMOS gates per state have 620mm² aggregate drawn gate active area before any diffusion spacing, MIM, switch, routing, shared bias or amplifier layout; this is not a cell footprint estimate.

With the sensor initially unpowered and both its 1.8V supply and 0.7V tail bias enabled at 4.99ms, the TT differential state remains similar but both rails jump from about 0.900V to about 1.422V. A 10ns supply/bias ramp and a 1µs ramp give nearly the same ~522mV common-mode step by 5ms; a supply-only enable with the tail held off also gives a large step. This points to readout-bias coupling into the tiny floating state, but the coupling path has not been isolated. The direct power-gated sensor is rejected as a read path. Source hashes and exact values are in `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_passive_gate_sensor_boundary.json`. Continue with a physical passive gate-cap/store and an isolated, prebiased shared read connection; quantify connect kickback, gate-cap voltage dependence, noise/mismatch, event timing and complete population service before any macro promotion.

The existing single 10×1µm NFET MOS gate-cap primitive passes Magic DRC0 and unique Netgen LVS; its earlier TT 0.9V AC screen estimated 77.32fF. In the new fixed-bus transient, placing that extracted MOSCAP on each rail reproduces the fully unpowered schematic-input-gate retention at TT, SS and FF to the reported voltage precision. The MOSCAP, MIM pair and TG are separately qualified layouts, not a routed combined state cell. Its source and qualification hashes are now in the passive-gate boundary report.

A first shared-read screen kept the schematic differential sensor continuously biased and precharged its input gates to 0.9V, then isolated them and connected the passive state through two extracted TGs at4.99ms. At TT/27°C the state differential was1.6155mV immediately before connection and0.8206mV at5ms, only50.8% retained across that read interval; common mode rose4.21mV. The sensor produced9.93mV output differential by5ms. This demonstrates an output response but rejects the direct connection as nondestructive readout. Its source hash and calculated fractions are in the same boundary report. The sensor, precharge controls and read timing are still schematic/ideal, with no noise, mismatch or full-float update.

Reducing only the schematic sensor input pair from10×1µm to the screened minimum0.42×1µm with the same extracted read TGs raises read-interval differential retention to92.53% (1.6155→1.4948mV). The sensor still produces3.483mV differential output, but state common mode moves+4.76mV and the final differential is0.2968mV below the matched no-read passive store at5ms. This is an improvement, not a nondestructive microvolt-scale read; small-input mismatch and noise are unmeasured. The source hash is included in the passive-gate boundary report.

An ideal 10Ω read-switch control with the same0.42µm sensor inputs leaves1.7385mV at5ms, versus1.7916mV with no read. Its preconnect differential is1.7920mV, whereas the extracted off read TG already loads that state down to1.6155mV before it opens. The extracted read TG then leaves1.4948mV and adds the+4.76mV common-mode step. Thus the physical read switch accounts for most of the measured total differential penalty, while even ideal charge sharing costs about53µV here. The ideal switch is a diagnostic bound, not a realizable nondestructive path. All three source hashes are in the passive-gate report.

## Destructive boundary-read transfer screen — 2026-10-05

Reading the membrane at the bin boundary can disturb it if a writeback immediately follows, provided the *pre-read* state is recoverable within the model's accuracy and 5ms service budget. The preceding nondestructive-read rejection should therefore be read as a rejection of that particular direct read **as a nondestructive cell**, not of every destructive-read architecture. A minimum 0.42/0.84µm schematic read TG reduces the TT ±1mV end-state penalty only modestly against the existing extracted1/2µm TG; this does not justify minimum-TG layout on its own.

With the existing extracted read TG, physical MIM and physical 10×1µm passive MOSCAP, and a schematic 0.42µm-input shared sensor, commanded TT rail offsets −1/0/+1mV produce −3.4813/0/+3.4828mV output differential. The +1mV output is +2.5261mV at SS/0°C and +4.2241mV at FF/85°C; it reaches +34.8839mV at TT +10mV and +879.9545mV at TT +300mV. These deterministic points preserve sign, but seven state/corner cases do not prove a monotone, invertible transfer across the complete membrane range, nor a per-corner calibration mechanism. The sensor and precharge are schematic; the state primitives are separately extracted, not a routed combined macro.

The same 0.42µm sensor's stationary 10Hz–100kHz noise screen reports 0.5733mV RMS output noise. Dividing by the local ±1mV rail-offset transfer gives an illustrative164.65µV RMS rail-equivalent noise. A 10µm input device in the standalone sensor has42.58µV RMS input-referred noise over that band, but loads the stored state much more on connection. Neither PSD is a switched readout/noise-yield result. An optimistic full-float replay used ideal six-range encoding, independent165µV threshold noise, no measured retention/read/write error, and three seeds on each of two complete validation frames. Mean IoU was0.2001–0.2028 versus0.2057 float for frame0 and0.1981–0.2013 versus0.1989 float for frame1; pixel agreement with float was about80.7–82.3%, and final-fusion spike mismatch about3.7–4.0%. This is a task-sensitivity clue, not accuracy acceptance. All source hashes, exact transfer values, and replay limits are in `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_destructive_read_boundary.json`.

The direct sensor's high-range gain collapses. At TT, +450/+750/+900mV commanded rail offsets yield about+1.0186/+1.0556/+1.0568V output differential. The 750→900mV local slope is0.008246mV output per mV rail offset, versus3.482mV/mV near zero, about422× smaller. These are completed deterministic PDK/PEX state screens but only sparse samples; do not infer global monotonicity or exact resolution between points. A full-range destructive read would require a physically implemented range selector or level shift before the sensor, then calibration, switched noise, writeback and 5ms population timing. The ideal six-range replay above does not provide that circuit.

## Shared physical high-range read capacitor — 2026-10-05

A differential bank of five 10×10µm SKY130 MIM units per sensor rail is now routed in GDS with READP, READN and common VCM ports. Its33.62×63.505µm bounding box passes Magic DRC0 and unique Netgen LVS; extraction finds10 physical MIM devices and5 explicit coupling capacitors. The separately qualified unit measures206.58fF at TT. The bank's PEX has an internal parasitic `SUB` node absent from its port list; the transient grounds that node in a diagnostic include, while leaving standard PDK MIM devices and the extracted coupling capacitors intact. Physical substrate routing and the bank's sensor/selector assembly remain open.

At TT/27°C, +750→+900mV commanded rail-offset output slope is0.11815mV/mV with ideal1pF inputs,0.11474 with five separate physical MIM units,0.11036 with the routed-bank PEX, and0.10982 when two extracted selector TGs attach that bank before read. The direct no-cap sensor's slope over the same interval is only0.008246mV/mV. With the selector off, ±1mV low-range outputs are−3.3915/+3.3929mV versus−3.4813/+3.4828mV without a bank selector. With the selector on, ±900mV high-range outputs are−205.61/+212.18mV; signed asymmetry remains. These are isolated deterministic points and cannot establish a continuous calibrated inverse or physical range-selection controller.

The high-range read draws the small state toward common mode and needs writeback. Under the *diagnostic* serial schedule of10µs precharge plus10µs read per state,30,976,000 visits per5ms require at least123,904 parallel channels at zero overhead. Direct replication of this bank's GDS bounding box would occupy264.54mm² before state storage, access/read switches, sensor, clock, routing, calibration or writeback. These are conditional schedule/area calculations, not chip PPA or a proven service design. Source hashes, completed cases and exact calculations are in `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json`; GDS and qualification are under `artifacts/analog_asic/layout/sky130_shared_read_mim_bank_5x10x10/`.
2026-10-05 high-range PVT/hold correction: The shared physical five-MIM-per-rail read bank remains DRC0/LVS-unique as a capacitor module, but the selected high-range sensor cannot yet be promoted as a resident state solution. At SS/0C, commanded +750/+900mV rails yield 1100.33/1293.42mV pre-read differential and 137.79/161.13mV sensor output. At FF/85C, the +750mV case yields 952.45mV pre-read and 203.53mV output. FF +900mV finishes ngspice and gives 980.02mV pre-read/207.70mV output, but its initial state is about 1.709V differential rather than commanded 1.8V and fails the bench guard; do not use that endpoint as a qualified transfer point or its apparent slope as a sensor gain. The FF +750mV state retains only 1379.73/1491.43=92.51% after a shortened 100us visit before read. Holding the same 20us precharge/read diagnostic schedule for each 100us visit would require 6,195,200 zero-overhead parallel channels, with 13,227mm2 of the read-bank GDS bounding box alone; this is a conditional service illustration, not a proven minimum because shorter settle windows and better state topology have not been explored. Correctly precharging the floating middle node of two extracted access TGs and attaching the physical MOS gate-cap gives 1132.81mV differential after 5ms at FF +750mV, compared with 1091.91mV for one TG. The improvement is insufficient to establish full-range retention. Source hashes, numeric cases and caveats: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json. The next gate is lower-leak physical state/access, then sensor calibration and writeback.
2026-10-05 thick-oxide isolation follow-up: With the same routed 2x2um MIM pair and physical 10x1um MOS gate-cap per rail, but schematic PDK 5V TG devices (1um/0.5um) at FF/85C and a +750mV rail command, the 5ms final differential is 1200.15mV at ordinary off gates (0/1.8V) and 1282.61mV with off gates -0.5/2.3V. Increasing off-gate drive to -1.0/2.8V yields 1282.93mV, essentially no further benefit. The high rail still falls about 75.4mV and the low rail rises about 141.6mV at the latter setting. These are schematic switch models with ideal charge-pump voltages, not physical HV TG layout or reliability qualification. The plateau suggests that gate-controlled channel leakage is not the only limit of this tiny state cell; junction and parasitic paths need explicit separation before choosing a new isolation device. Source-hashed report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.
2026-10-05 asymmetric isolation rejection: A schematic thick-oxide NMOS-only path on the low rail and PMOS-only path on the high rail, each with the same physical MOS gate-cap and ideal -1.0/2.8V off gates, ended at 1526.00mV differential from 1500.01mV initially for +750mV at FF/85C. At SS/0C the same high-range pairing ended at 1528.18mV. However the FF +1mV near-zero state started at 2.02mV differential and ended at 100.48mV after 5ms; the NMOS-only side drifts -49.45mV and PMOS-only side +49.02mV. Reject a fixed NMOS-low/PMOS-high pairing as a signed membrane store. This is a deterministic schematic diagnostic with extracted MIM/MOS gate-cap but no physical HV access cell, event injector, read/writeback, mismatch/noise or full-model validation. It does not justify a sign-dependent device selector because attaching both device types would restore their leakage paths. Source-hashed report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.
2026-10-05 signed-polarity check: The same fixed asymmetric HV cell at FF/85C and reversed -750mV rails starts at -1499.94mV differential and ends at -1250.65mV after 5ms, while the positive state ends at +1526.00mV. This strong polarity dependence compounds its +100.48mV false near-zero state; no full signed transfer or calibrated inverse has been established. Continue treating it as a rejected fixed storage cell, not a signed PLIF candidate. Updated source-hashed report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.
2026-10-06 open-PDK readiness recheck: The official ICsprout55 repository still lists SPICE models and RC extraction among unfinished deliverables (https://github.com/openecos-projects/icsprout55-pdk). The official OpenRPDK28 README still says its 28nm PDK is under construction (https://github.com/RIOSLaboratory/OpenRPDK28). IHP SG13G2 remains a real analog-capable 130nm preview PDK with MIM and MOS models (https://github.com/IHP-GmbH/IHP-Open-PDK), but its node and published capacitor density do not demonstrate a smaller resident state cell than the current SKY130 physical evidence. Therefore keep the SKY130 module bench and architecture gate; there is no newly source-backed ready small-node port that resolves the measured leakage/noise/service problem. This is a public-collateral screen as of this date, not a foundry capability claim.
2026-10-06 held-node leakage isolation: In the FF/85C +750mV, 5ms schematic 5V-TG screen, parking each overdriven off-switch source at its own initially commanded state instead of the fixed 0.9V shared bus leaves the 1V-overdriven TG low/high drift essentially unchanged: +141.6126/-75.4050mV on VCM and +141.6117/-75.4050mV parked. The NMOS-only and PMOS-only overdriven variants are likewise unchanged to the reported precision. By contrast, ordinary 0/1.8V off gates change the low-rail drift from +222.99mV to +12.71mV when parked. This separates a channel-voltage-sensitive component removed by off-gate overdrive from a remaining held-node leakage path; junction/body or model parasitics are plausible, but not proven without branch-current accounting. A fixed shared bus is the realistic comparison; parked per-state buses are diagnostic only. Do not pursue more simple gate overdrive. Next isolate transistor terminal currents and seek a storage node without a continuously reverse-biased diffusion before layout promotion. Source-hashed report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.
2026-10-06 HV terminal-current diagnosis: Zero-volt probes were inserted at the body, off-bus and stored-node terminals of the schematic 5V TG without changing its FF/85C +750mV 5ms drift at reported precision. At 5ms the overdriven low-rail NMOS has body/source/state probe magnitudes 1.212/0.910/0.309pA, and the PMOS 2.426/0.910/1.512pA. On the high rail they are NMOS 2.494/0.910/1.581pA and PMOS 1.147/0.910/0.240pA. The signed body current agrees with the source-plus-state terminal currents within about 0.006pA for these devices. This directly locates the surviving pA path at the modeled body terminals, consistent with reverse-biased junction leakage; the gate terminal and transient capacitance are not independently probed. At the low stored node the PMOS state current overwhelms the opposing NMOS current; at the high node the NMOS dominates, explaining the 5ms compression. A viable compact store needs to remove or greatly reduce those continuously connected diffusion/body paths, not just add off-gate voltage. These are schematic 5V devices and diagnostic extracted MIM/MOS gate-cap, not a qualified physical HV cell. Source-hashed terminal values: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.

2026-10-06 diode-state cross-simulator correction: A standalone 1×1µm P+–Nwell diode primitive has a 3.65×3.65µm GDS bbox, Magic DRC0, unique Netgen LVS, and extracted SPICE; the Nwell-to-substrate junction remains a drawn-geometry PDK proxy. The extracted PDK diode forward I–V differs radically between the installed simulators: ngspice36 reports only 1.66fA at 1V and 4.99fA at 3V, whereas ngspice47 reports 2.97nA at 0.5V and 184.5µA at 1V on the identical PEX/model deck. The ngspice36 5ms near-perfect hold and failed-write reports are therefore simulator-specific and cannot qualify the state. With ngspice47, ideal 20ns bus pulses at +2.2/−0.4V program the selected differential from +2mV to +1248.54mV and then −1248.52mV, but an intentionally connected neighbor changes identically; there is no address selection. At FF/85C, the physical diode PEX plus provisional well proxy retains only 1242.546mV of 1499.933mV after 5ms for a +750mV rail offset (82.84%), and 1.724mV of 2mV near zero (86.2%). Reject this direct two-diode shared-bus resident membrane topology. The useful diode primitive and model-version diagnostic remain; they do not qualify programming selectivity, membrane accuracy, read/writeback, noise/mismatch, 5ms inventory service, or full-chip layout. Source-hashed files: `artifacts/analog_asic/layout/sky130_diode_pd2nw_05v5_probe_1x1/qualification.json`, `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_diode_forward_iv_tt27_extracted_vs_control_ngspice47.json`, `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_diode_state_write_tt27_plus2p2_minusneg0p4_width20ns_ngspice47.json`, and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_diode_isolated_mim_state_ff_temp85_offset{750,1}mv_area1um2_perim4um_wellproxy_physicaldiode_ngspice47.json`. The official, still-open diode-model compatibility PR is https://github.com/google/skywater-pdk-libs-sky130_fd_pr/pull/5; it is context, not an accepted replacement model.
The extracted 1/2µm transmission-gate state screen was also rerun with ngspice47 at FF/85C using the same reduced MIM pair and diagnostic substrate ground: 0.6/1.2V commanded states begin at0.580301/1.18989V and end after5ms at0.791701/1.08087V. The ideal-switch control stays at0.6/1.2V. This independent switch topology therefore still fails small-state retention with the newer simulator. Shortening the physical-diode/well-proxy FF/85C +750mV hold to100µs leaves1494.055mV from1499.933mV, a5.878mV (0.392%) differential loss. At that visit interval,30,976,000 resident states imply309.76 billion state visits per second before any event processing, sensing, writeback, or refresh control. This is a conditional refresh-rate calculation, not a completed service design; the 100µs loss itself is not an accepted original-float state error. Source-hashed short-window report: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_diode_isolated_mim_state_ff_temp85_offset750mv_area1um2_perim4um_wellproxy_physicaldiode_ngspice47_hold100us.json`.
2026-10-06 equal-charge event-time screen: The extracted diode plus physical MIM/MOS-gate-cap and provisional Nwell-to-substrate PDK junction proxy was driven by equal ideal 102aC-per-rail signed charge events (50ns flat top, 51ns trapezoid) at0.5ms or4.5ms, with a matching no-event control. At FF/85C near a±1mV rail offset, the early event adds2.124mV immediately and1.860mV at5ms; the late event adds2.098mV immediately and2.068mV at5ms. Its early/late final contribution ratio is0.8994. At a±750mV rail offset, the early event adds3.025mV immediately and2.193mV at5ms; the late event adds2.788mV immediately and2.697mV at5ms, ratio0.8131. Thus even an ideal equal-charge injector sees materially state-dependent event-time weighting. A single fixed timing correction inferred near zero cannot be assumed to preserve both ranges or original-float summation. These are ideal current-source diagnostics, not a physical injector or closed-loop correction. They reinforce rejection of direct diode-state replication. Source-hashed reports: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_diode_event_time_ff85_ngspice47.json` and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_diode_event_time_ff85_ngspice47_offset750mv.json`.

2026-10-06 full-frame state occupancy gate: Replayed two checkpoint-listed validation records through the verified epoch-58, full-float EventSpikeSeg140k at440×640 and10 updates with QAT disabled. All25 executed PLIF actors account for exactly30,976,000 resident states; the26th registered PLIF module is outside the executed graph. Exact nonzero post-reset membranes rise from75.22% to82.06% on record0 and75.93% to82.45% on record1 from update1 to update10. At update10 this is25,418,466 and25,540,473 nonzero states respectively. Even an ideal exact-zero sparse allocation would retain at least82% of physical analog values on these records, before tags, addressing, allocation, routing, concurrent event service or reconstruction. The final-fusion actor alone has4,505,600 resident states and is99.12%/99.99% nonzero at update10 on the two records. This rejects a blanket "most states are zero" premise as a route around dense analog storage; it does not prove dataset-wide occupancy or rule out a separately validated stage-specific optimization. Reproducible script: `scripts/analyze_sky130_float_state_occupancy.py`; machine-readable report: `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_float_fullframe_state_occupancy.json`.
Differential area interpretation: the earlier1,101mm² routed small-MIM-pair replication floor assumed its two capacitors store **two single-ended logical states**. The active differential membrane uses both rails for **one** logical state, so one routed23.62×3.01µm pair per30,976,000 states has a2,202.276mm² direct bbox replication floor; its two bare2×2µm plates have a247.808mm² geometric plate floor. Neither includes access, state sensor, injector, spacing, power or routing, and the cell already fails5ms retention. The summary JSON now carries both mappings to avoid applying the historical single-ended floor to this architecture.
